The Section of the Mantra Ritual of Śrī Paramādya (Śrīparamādyamantrakalpakhaṇḍa)

The Section of the Mantra Ritual of Śrī Paramādya, known in Sanskrit as the Śrī Paramādya Mantra Kalpa Khaṇḍa and in Tibetan as Pal Chok Dangpö Ngak kyi Tokpé Dumbu, is a tantra devoted to the cycle of Paramādya, the Glorious Supreme Primordial One, a form of Vajrasattva identified with great bliss. Opening with Vairocana teaching the Pledge of the Unfailing Vajra of Great Bliss as a gateway to the Perfection of Wisdom, it presents great desire, when wholly purified, as the path to supreme accomplishment, and sets out mantras, mudrās, maṇḍala rites and the practice of the deities of the vajra families, closing with a series of questions and answers on the meaning of its own rites. It is Toh 488 in the Kangyur, where it belongs to the Tantra section, among the yoga tantras. According to its colophon it was translated at Tholing by the Indian paṇḍita Mantrakalaśa and the royal monk Zhiwa Ö, with gaps left in an earlier translation by Rinchen Zangpo later filled from manuscripts that had since been found.

In the language of India: śrī baramādya mantra kalpa khaṇḍa nāma. In the language of Tibet: The Section of the Mantra Ritual of Śrī Paramādya.

Homage to the Blessed One, Great Bliss.

Then the Blessed One Vairocana, who has realized the secret nature of reality of all the tathāgatas, because all phenomena are free of elaboration, once again spoke this sublime teaching called the Pledge of the Unfailing Vajra of Great Bliss, the gateway of the Perfection of Wisdom that is the vajra nature of reality, Śrī Paramādya, the Glorious Supreme Primordial One, without beginning, end or middle:

The supreme accomplishment of great desire of the great bodhisattvas becomes the supreme accomplishment of great bliss. The great bodhisattvas' supreme accomplishment of great bliss becomes the supreme accomplishment of the great awakening of all the tathāgatas. The supreme accomplishment of the great awakening of all the tathāgatas becomes the supreme accomplishment that utterly crushes all the power of the great Māra. The bodhisattvas' supreme accomplishment of utterly crushing the power of the great Māra becomes the supreme accomplishment of great sovereignty over all the three realms. The bodhisattvas' supreme accomplishment of sovereignty over all that belongs to the three realms, because it is the cause of their wish to purify completely the realm of sentient beings, entirely and without exception, and because the bodhisattvas of great diligence, unattached even to saṃsāra, completely protect the realm of sentient beings, entirely and without exception, becomes the supreme accomplishment of benefit and happiness for all sentient beings, the highest and utterly great bliss.

Why is that so?

As long as the supremely wise
Remain in the abode of saṃsāra,
So long are they able, unequaled, to work for beings
Without passing into nirvāṇa.

Through the perfection of wisdom and means,
And by making primordial wisdom their abode,
Since all phenomena are completely pure,
Saṃsāra becomes completely pure.

If they tame by means of desire and the rest,
Then constantly, as long as saṃsāra lasts,
To purify those in the world who do evil,
They themselves will tame them for as long as saṃsāra endures.

Just as a lotus, deep red,
Is not stained by the fault of the mud,
So those who benefit beings
Are not stained by the faults of their abode.

Great desire, completely pure,
Great bliss and great wealth:
Because they have attained lordship over the three realms,
They teach for the benefit of beings.

Whoever rises in the morning and every day recites, hears, retains, reads and meditates on this Dharma discourse, the gateway of the way of the Perfection of Wisdom of Śrī Paramādya, will obtain all happiness and joy of mind. In this very life he will also obtain the supreme accomplishment of the Pledge of the Unfailing Vajra of Great Bliss. He will also accomplish the supreme secret of all the tathāgatas, and will become all the great vajra holders, or a tathāgata.

From the Pledge of the Unfailing Vajra of Great Bliss, Śrī Paramādya, the gateway of the Perfection of Wisdom of the vajra secret of great bliss of all the tathāgatas is complete.

Then the Blessed One, the great Tathāgata Vairocana, the great king of the extensive rite for the accomplishment of the supreme vajra secret blessed by Vajrasattva of all the tathāgatas, the vajra of all body, speech and mind sealed by the rite that is the unfailing teaching of the vajra secret of the body, speech and mind of all the tathāgatas, who had fully entered into all the great pledges of all the tathāgatas and fully entered into the primordial wisdom that realizes the nature of reality, and whose action reaches the very limit, emanating through the manifold activities of all the tathāgatas into all world systems without exception in the three times, was dwelling in the celestial palace that is the abode of Vajrasattva, Great Bliss. It shone with the light of the manifold rays blazing from vajras and jewels set upon many half-moons. It was beautified by hanging golden bells and by manifold banners stirred by the wind. It was lovely and adorned with hanging garlands of various flowers, with cloths, mirrors and strings of pearls. It was richly adorned with supreme goddesses standing on its various balconies, with their laughter, their grace, their dancing and their songs, and with praises that awaken.

Then the Blessed One Vajrasattva, supreme among all the pledge beings blessed by the vajra of the body, speech and mind of all the tathāgatas, among all the empowerments, and among all the meditative absorptions and actions of omniscience, the tamer of all the three realms, the one who brings all under control, the holder, possessor of all the rites of the vast accomplishment of every desire, made this supplication to the eternal Tathāgata:

"As long as existence remains,
So long, accomplish the supreme aim of beings.
Because the realm of beings is boundless,
And because the Victor's children choose existence,
In order to destroy the faults of existence,
And in order to increase great bliss,
Grant me the authorization
Of the supreme secret rite that you have spoken."

Then the Blessed One, the Tathāgata, contemplating intently the very unobscured nature of all phenomena, entered into absorption in the meditative absorption called the Manifold Vajra of Great Compassion for All Who Are to Be Tamed, which completely fulfills all the wishes of sentient beings, entirely and without exception, protects them all, and brings about every excellence of benefit, happiness and accomplishment, up to becoming the cause of primordial wisdom, of action, and of supreme accomplishment. The one who had attained all the tathāgatas themselves and the great Vajradhara, the first vajra of great bliss whose aim is supreme accomplishment, the universal monarch of all the tathāgatas, was himself consecrated with the empowerment of the diadem of the garland of the bodies of all the tathāgatas.

Then, in order to obtain all accomplishments, beginning with the supreme accomplishment of great bliss, entire and without exception, the Blessed One, the great Vajradhara, sat in the cross-legged posture of a sattva, held his body straight, set his left hand in the gesture of pride, and, twirling the vajra in his right, held at his heart the application of the first and supreme vajra. And this very mudrā of all, called the Vajra of Great Bliss, issued from his body, speech and mind:

oṃ mahāsukha vajrasatva jaḥ hūṃ baṃ hoḥ surata stvaṃ.

As soon as it had issued forth, all the tathāgatas came, and those who had fully taken up the fist of all the tathāgatas once again fully awakened to great awakening, and they burst into great laughter. All the world systems quaked and became completely pure. All the vast elaborations of the three realms became the great maṇḍala of the Blessed One, through which all sentient beings obtained happiness and joy of mind.

Then the great Vajradhara entered into absorption in the meditative absorption called the Great Vajra Mudrā, arisen from the unfailing and unobstructed nature of all the tathāgatas. Bringing under his sway what is called the Pledge of the Vajra of Great Bliss, he emitted from his whole body the beings of the Pledge of the Vajra of Great Bliss, who bestow all accomplishments, happiness and joy of mind, who are constantly joined with him and act together with him, and sent them out in all directions. In front was the Vajra Secret Born from Mind; on the right, Vajrakilikila; behind, Kāmavajra; and on the left, Vajragarvā. In the corners were the offering goddesses. At the corners of the outer parapet were the goddesses. The gatekeepers were those holding the vajra hook, noose, chain and bell. Outside were the hosts of vajra gatekeepers, the messengers and the servants.

Then the Blessed One, the Tathāgata, binding the fist of all the tathāgatas, entered into absorption in the meditative absorption called the Awakening Vajra of All the Tathāgatas, and made this supplication to the Blessed One Vajrasattva, the great Vajradhara:

"For the happiness of all beings
The great victors come forth;
Free of desire, yet possessed of desire,
They partake even of their own consorts.
Therefore I request of you,
Great bliss, great vajra:
The accomplishment of the secret rite,
The supreme discipline, please explain."

Then the Blessed One, bringing under his sway the unfailing and unobstructed command called the Supreme Vajra Desire, the discipline of every accomplishment of great bliss, spoke this great maṇḍala called the Pledge of the Great Vajra Secret:

"Next I shall rightly explain
The great and sublime maṇḍala.
Great bliss, the supreme vajra,
Bears the name of the sublime pledge.

Having prepared the site of the maṇḍala,
In its center, according to the rite,
Settled in the great mudrā,
One should accomplish the maṇḍala there.

With a red cord, new, well twisted,
And without any knots,
One should lay out the lines,
And draw it with colored powders.

Four-cornered, with four gates,
Beautified by four archways,
Encircled all around by a parapet,
Its corners lovely with goddesses.

At the corners of the maṇḍala,
And at the corners of gates and archways,
Vajras and jewels set on half-moons
Blaze with rays of light.

Supreme bells stirred by the wind
And various banners make it lovely.
Furnished with mirrors, nets and garlands of flowers,
One should draw the outer maṇḍala.

Within it, a wheel
Resembling an eightfold maṇḍala,
Encircled by vajra lines:
One should rightly draw the vajra wheel.

In its center, according to the rite,
One should set Vajrasattva,
Cast in metal, or else fashioned,
Or hammered, or else painted,

Smiling, with the light of the moon,
Pervaded by blazing red rays,
Beautified by a garland of buddhas,
His body well anointed with colors,

Seated in the cross-legged posture of a sattva,
Supreme one holding the vajra in pride.
Saying samaya stvaṃ,
One should set him at the heart.

On every side around him
One should draw the great beings,
With paints or else with colored powders,
Each in its proper order.

In front, the one red in color,
Intent on the vajra weapon;
On the right, the one of pure white,
Holding the vajra in close embrace;

Behind, the one yellow in color,
Holding the makara banner;
On the left, the one blue in color,
Both hands held in pride, and the rest.

Holding vessels of offering,
The offering goddesses should be drawn:
The Graceful One, the Laughing One, the Singer
And the Dancer, the hosts of goddesses.

Vajra hook and noose,
Vajra chain and bell:
At the four gates
One should set the four gatekeepers."

Then their heart mantras are these:

hūṃ vajradriṣṭa sāmake maṭ hūṃ vajra kili kilāya hūṃ hūṃ smara vajra bandha hūṃ hūṃ tuṣṭa vajriṇi hūṃ.

These are the vajra servants.

Then, those of the offering goddesses: hūṃ madhura vajri hūṃ hūṃ vajra mighe hūṃ hūṃ śārad vajri hūṃ hūṃ vajra śiśiri hūṃ.

Then, those of the goddesses: hūṃ vajra lāsyai mahābar vajriṇi oṃ vajra nrityai.

Then, those of the gatekeepers: jaḥ vajra aṃkuśi pratihara hūṃ vajra pāśa gana baṃ vajra sphoṭa duha aḥ vajra ghanḍaccheda.

Thus, in the maṇḍala of the goddesses,
One should set those renowned women themselves.
When they have been set in place,
It becomes blessed by them.

Then the one who holds the vajra in hand,
Having drawn the maṇḍala supreme over all,
Should worship with as many full vases as he can,
And with flowers and every other offering.
Banners hung with bells
Should be set at the corners.
Vases with sprigs of leaves
Should be set outside the gates.
With the vajra, and with the four,
Having recited over the sprig,
A vase filled with urine
Should be set before Vajrapāṇi.

Here the heart mantra that blesses the nectar water of the pledge of great bliss is this: oṃ mahāsukha vajra samaya abhiṣeka jaḥ hūṃ baṃ hoḥ. It is recited twenty-one times. If this water is sprinkled or drunk, all suffering is thoroughly removed and all happiness greatly increases.

Then, by means of their own pledges, one should accomplish Vajradhara and the rest. Saying jaḥ hūṃ baṃ hoḥ:

Summoning, causing to enter,
And binding, in an instant they come under control.
Settled in the great mudrā,
Make the right index finger likewise into a hook.
This summoning succeeds
At a single try; then one should cause them to enter fully.

oṃ mahāsukha vajrasatva a ahi śīghraṃ mahāsukha vajra guhya samaya manusmara phrabhuta samaya staṃ hṛdayaṃ sutoṣyo me bhaba supoṣyo me bhaba anurakto me bheba adhitiṣṭha bhagabāna anatinaṃ dana sid dhyaṃ tu me mantrapadaiḥ jaḥ hūṃ baṃ hoḥ.

Then the Blessed One himself
Always comes into the maṇḍala.
Summoned, made to enter and made firm,
He comes under control, and he is accomplished.

One sees many wondrous miracles, or else one will see the Blessed One himself.

Then the vajra holder himself, master of all, has accomplished all the mudrās, knows all the pledges, is capable of all actions, and is blessed by the Blessed One. By this rite of entry, the vajra master himself, anointed and perfumed with as many fragrances as there are, his body richly adorned with garlands of flowers together with ornaments, and clad in two red garments, should himself praise the Blessed One and should himself enter, in the pride of Vajragarvā, holding the vajra. The praise is to be sung in the ṣaḍja mode and the like:

"You whose mind is every bliss, desire and the rest,
All-encompassing one, grant bliss to me!
Vajrasattva, think of me, and quickly show yourself to me!
You who delight me, perfect in supreme bliss, to you I bow."

Then, having fully entered, one should make this request:

"Being without beginning or end,
Vajrasattva, great bliss,
You are Samantabhadra,
Lord of vajra pride, all-pervading lord.
Meditative absorption of the being of mind,
Likewise vajra, holder of the vajra,
Possessor of the accomplishment of the pledge supreme over all,
Vajra mind, accomplish me today!"

Then the Blessed One himself will bless the vajra master. Then a bliss of individual self-awareness, never known before, will arise in both. The subtle will also descend. Then one should know, "The Blessed One has been accomplished for me." Then, remaining just so, one should do whatever recitation one wishes. Then, remaining just so, one will obtain the desired accomplishment through the blessing of one's own merit. From then on, all the great beings will be accomplished and will be seen, and they too will grant him every accomplishment. If one binds all the mudrās oneself, all actions will be accomplished. All the pledges will also be revealed to him. He also performs the actions his mind desires. Experiencing every happiness and joy of mind, unconquerable by others and not to be slain, after one year has passed he obtains the supreme accomplishment, buddhahood and the rest.

Then one should examine the disciples for the purpose of bringing all beings in. The vajra master himself, having adorned his body as before, should place the mudrā of the pledge of the vajra of utmost bliss, and likewise, rightly urging with the urging of the great reality, should summon. He says this:

"For the sake of the purity of the bliss of existence
You delighted in existence before.
Great accomplishment of every desire,
Bring it about for all beings too."

Then, having made offerings, one should cause the pledge mudrā of whichever is one's own special deity to enter, together with a flower. Having made it enter, one should say this into the ear:

"The world to be tamed through dispassion
Is tamed by dispassion itself.
Great desire, great bliss,
Is Vajrasattva hidden.
There is, by nature, no other suffering
Like that of being parted from desire.
Therefore, O being, do not once again
Turn your mind toward dispassion."

Then one should have him cast the flower and the rest with this heart mantra: oṃ mahāsukha vajrasatva bratigrihṇa samaya stvaṃ. Then wherever it falls, that is his deity. Then one should declare the great reality. One gives him whatever heart mantra he desires and has him practice it; and oneself, having consecrated him with the empowerment of the special deity, places that deity's emblem in his two hands, confers on him the name empowerment of that family, and should empower him with this heart mantra: oṃ sarba tathāgata ātmaki 'haṃ vajradhāra samūkhebha abhiṣega tvāṃ abhiṣiñcāmi gṛhṇa siddhi vajriti amogha siddhi stvaṃ.

Then one should teach the binding of the mudrās. Then, having made whatever offerings are desired and had them made, and having satisfied the disciples with whatever food and drink they wish, one should have them perform mudrās and answering mudrās, dancing and laughter, and should do so oneself as well. Then, having sent everyone away, one should say this:

"This world, which wanders from beginning to end,
Depends upon supreme joy;
It becomes the great being, the vajra.
Please depart once more."

Then, having cast away the flowers held in the cupped palms, one should send them out. As for the maṇḍala, one should sweep it up oneself; if one makes pills from those colored powders and keeps them, all rites will be accomplished. Then, if one also introduces that procedure of the nectar of the Great Bliss Vajra Samaya, all rites, such as increasing life, are intended. If one empowers with that very nectar, one obtains empowerment into the maṇḍala and also obtains the supreme accomplishment. If one cannot find an artist to draw the figures, one should draw the maṇḍala with the mudrās of their respective hand emblems. If one does not know even that, one should set out the hand emblems. This is the extensive rite of the mudrās.

Then the Blessed One entered the samādhi called the Vajra Samaya of the Vast Rite of Swift, Unobstructed, and Meaningful Accomplishment, and, having exercised his overpowering, the great Vajradhara spoke this:

"For the full accomplishment of great bliss
And for the purification of great desire,
I shall explain the accomplishment of Vajrasattva,
Which is ever swift.

As long as one is in the abode of saṃsāra,
For so long should one deliver sentient beings.
Nirvāṇa obscures awakening;
Therefore these are the samayas:
As long as one remains in existence,
For so long one acts for the benefit of beings.

Possessing the supreme quality of utter purity,
Just as this fully accomplishes all accomplishments
For the benefit of all sentient beings,
Not so do the perfections, such as giving,
Through the supreme conduct of awakening.
The tathāgatas, toward those not yet tamed,
Act for the benefit of sentient beings;
When this accomplishment has been fully achieved,
They do everything at all times.

Therefore I shall explain this practice:
It is an accomplishment that is utterly permanent.
Through it, in this very existence,
One gains the utterly permanent accomplishment.

Even if one has not entered the maṇḍalas,
Or has altogether abandoned the samayas,
Or even lacks merit, in every way
One quickly succeeds in this existence.

If one wishes to achieve accomplishment,
And is always endowed with the objects of the senses,
Having worshiped with the secret offerings,
One will succeed through one's own mudrā.

With sweet songs, such as those in the ṣaḍja mode,
And with every passion, just as one wishes,
And with recitation, as much as one is able,
One should accomplish Vajrasattva.

By this one becomes lord of all desires,
Comes and goes at will, and travels through the sky;
One attains lordship over all accomplishments,
Is free of aging and death, and becomes the chief.
Worshiped by all the victors,
Served and honored by all, oneself and others,
One is equal to the great Vajradhara,
And one's conduct is unfailing, supreme above all."

The king of the accomplishment of the Great Bliss Vajra, which fully bestows all accomplishments, such as that of a tathāgata.

If a bodhisattva who is a great Vajradhara truly wishes for the supreme accomplishment of all the tathāgatas, he should paint on cloth the Blessed One, the Tathāgata, holding the fist of the mudrā of all the tathāgatas, smiling, and surrounded on every side by tathāgatas. In front of it, holding the fist of all the tathāgatas, he should recite the vajra words one hundred thousand times. When the hundred thousand are complete, at midnight, hosts of māras will come. Then he should destroy them with the Crushing of the Power of All Māras. Then, at dawn, he will attain the very state of all the tathāgatas, or the very state of a bodhisattva.

If he truly wishes for the very state of the great Vajradhara, then the practice of Vajrasattva has been explained according to whatever particular image he obtained for the earlier practice, whether cast, assembled, hammered, or well painted on cloth and so forth, as in the maṇḍala of Vajrapāṇi. In six months the great accomplishment of the great Vajradhara is achieved. If he would summon the great Vajradhara, he should summon him with the Great Bliss Vajra Hook and accomplish whatever he wishes. After one month has passed, he should make every request.

If one accomplishes oneself through one's own mudrā, one should bind the great mudrā of one's own deity and, meditating, should practice while meditating, "I am Vajrasattva." One should accomplish oneself through recitation, just as one wishes. One should oneself meditate and recite again and again, for as long as it takes, until after half a month has passed one attains the very state of Vajradhara.

Then, if one wishes to accomplish lordship over all desires, one should in the same way fully enter together with one's beloved, bind the mudrā, and recite until one becomes lord of all desires and master of the entire three realms.

Then the Blessed One Vajradhara, having displayed the play of the wisdom of the nature of reality, in which the whole intrinsic nature of the vajra continuum of the entire three realms is accomplished, spoke this extensive vajra rite, the continuum of mudrā activities that require no prior accomplishment.

The heart-mantra activities that require no prior accomplishment are as follows. Protecting oneself is done with the Great Bliss Vajra Secret Uṣṇīṣa. Protecting everyone is done with the Great Bliss Vajra Secret Ūrṇā. Binding all the directions, the boundaries, and the maṇḍala is done with the Great Bliss Vajra Secret Blazing Garland. Crushing the power of all māras is done with the Great Bliss Vajra Secret Unshakable One. Increasing life is done with the Great Bliss Vajra Secret Nectar. Great and perfect beauty of form is gained with the Great Bliss Vajra Secret Embodied One. For the full increase of splendor and of excellent youth, it is done with the Great Bliss Vajra Secret Elixir. For increasing strength and complexion, it is done with the Great Bliss Vajra Secret Might. For all empowerments, it is done with the Vajra Secret Crowned One. For obtaining all wealth, it is done with the Vajra Secret Treasure. For obtaining kingship, it is done with the Vajra Secret King's Mudrā. For obtaining the entire earth, it is done with the Vajra Secret Prowess. For making all beings be possessed, tremble, and dance, and for making them speak the truth about the three times, it is done with the Great Bliss Vajra Secret Causing to Enter for the Sake of the Samaya. For accomplishing all divinations (prasenas), it is done with the Vajra Secret Descent of Prasena. For liberation from all grahas, it is done with the Vajra Secret Liberation from Grahas.

For destroying all the malevolent, it is done with the Vajra Secret Vanquisher of Obstructors. For paralyzing everyone, it is done with the Vajra Secret Paralysis. For making everyone fall asleep, it is done with the Vajra Secret Sleep. For sealing everyone, it is done with the Great Bliss Vajra Secret Samaya Ḍākinī. For secrecy in all things, it is done with the Vajra Secret Antaka (the Ender). For overthrowing everyone, it is done with the Vajra Secret Maker of hūṃ. For raising everyone up, it is done with the Vajra Secret Raising by the Samaya. For binding everyone, it is done with the Vajra Secret Binding. For freeing everyone, it is done with the Vajra Secret Syllable mu. For freeing from all sorceresses and restoring the former condition, it is done with the Great Secret Heruka Vajra. For liberating from all the grahas of the mātṛkās, it is done with the Vajra Secret Fierce Goddess. For freeing those sealed by any of the sorceresses, for causing another heart to arise in place of a heart that has been eaten, and for reviving the dead, it is done with the Great Samaya Secret Heruka Vajra Empowerment. For destroying the mudrās of all the malevolent, it is done with the Vajra Secret Vajra. For fully pacifying all fevers, it is done with the Vajra Secret Blazing Gaze. For destroying all poisons, for eating them, for making them flow out, and for reviving from death, it is done with the Great Secret Vajra Jewel Mudrā. For turning all poisons and all compounded poisons into nectar, it is done with the Vajra Secret Poisonless One. For destroying all slayers, sorcery, vetālas, harmful spirits of misfortune, and yakṣiṇīs, it is done with the Vajra Secret Fire Offering. For destroying all disputes, fights, battles, and contention, it is done with the Vajra Secret Wide-Open Mouth. For destroying epidemics, all harm, misdeeds, and bad dreams, it is done with the Vajra Secret Peace. For paralyzing all hostile armies, putting them to sleep, stupefying them, striking them mute, sealing them, and killing them, it is done with the Great Bliss Vajra Secret Slayer. For gaining victory in all battles, it is done with the Vajra Secret Victory-Banner Armlet. For destroying all the malevolent, it is done with the Vajra Secret Excellent Vajra. For pacifying all obstructors, it is done with the Vajra Secret Application of Means. For utterly destroying all enemies, it is done with the Vajra Secret Scattering. For crushing all the power of the māras, it is done with the Vajra Secret Complete Taming.

For producing clouds, it is done with the Vajra Secret Great Cloud. For bringing down rain, it is done with the Vajra Secret Threatening Finger. For pacifying thunderbolts, lightning, wind, and rain, it is done with the Vajra Secret Cloud Guard. For dispelling clouds, darkness, and mist, it is done with the Vajra Secret Eye of Great Bliss. For summoning all nāgas, it is done with the Vajra Secret Mudrā. For bringing all nāgas under control, it is done with the Mudrā of the Jewel of All Secrets. For making all nāgas follow, it is done with the Vajra Secret Taming of Nāgas. For driving out all nāgas, it is done with the Vajra Secret Expulsion of Nāgas. For making the whole earth tremble and quake, it is done with the Vajra Secret Liberation. For producing, where there is no water, a mass of water, a great rain, cool and stainless, it is done with the Great Bliss Vajra Nāga Mudrā. For producing the great light of the sun in darkness, it is done with the Vajra Sun Mudrā. For bringing all winds under control, it is done with the Vajra of All Winds. For displaying all forms in the sky, it is done with the Vajra Secret Holding. For making all things arise from the sky, it is done with the Vajra Secret Arising. For displaying one's own self everywhere in the sky, it is done with the Vajra Secret Miraculous Power. For displaying in the sky the tathāgatas, all the bodhisattvas such as the vajra holders, and all world systems, it is done with the Vajra Secret Magical Display. For making leaves, flowers, and all fruits appear on all trees in the sky, it is done with the Vajra Secret Season Mudrā. For making all kinds of grain ripen in every part of the land, it is done with the Vajra Secret Plow Mudrā. For producing from everything divine food, drink, clothing, ornaments, new dwellings, and young maidens, and for producing men and women and all male and female servants, it is done with the Water of Merit of the Great Bliss Vajra Samaya. For displaying the fourfold army in battle and waging war, it is done with the Vajra Restraint of Great Bliss. For displaying all illusions, it is done with the Vajra Saṃvara. For all the activities of Saṃvara, it is done with the Vajra Activity of Illusion. For displaying all marvels and miracles, it is done with the Vajra Magical Display of Marvelous Miraculous Power. For giving all answers, it is done with the Vajra Revealing of Answers. For paralyzing fire, it is done with the Vajra Utter Cold. For bringing down water, it is done with the Vajra Moon Crystal. For protecting hermitages, villages, forests, rivers, ponds, pleasure groves, food and drink, and temples, it is done with the Vajra Accomplishment. For the accomplishments of standing on water, displaying oneself in many forms, invisibility, and traveling through the sky, it is done with the Vajra Secret Mudrā of Accomplishment. For commanding all, the gods and the rest, it is done with the Vajra Secret Command. For bringing all sentient beings under control, it is done with the Vajra Secret Control. For drawing forth all immobile things, making them descend, and making them speak the truth, it is done with the Vajra Secret Mudrā of Great Accomplishment. For making all images descend, summoning them, making them laugh, making them dance, making them play, making them carry out every command, making them bring everything, and making them perform all tasks, it is done with the Accomplished Samaya of the Great Bliss Vajra. For making everyone impassioned, it is done with the Mind-Born of the Great Bliss Vajra. For making all moving and unmoving things play, it is done with the Vajra kilikila Secret. For making everything firm, it is done with the Vajra Makara Mudrā. For making everyone heroic, it is done with Vajragarvā.

For averting bad dreams, it is done with the Vajra Speech. For making others see the dreams one wishes, it is done with the Vajra Seeing of Dreams. For destroying all bad dreams, it is done with the Vajra Burnt Oblation. For accomplishing all good dreams and making them firm, it is done with the Vajra Accomplishment of Dreams. For dispelling all diseases and harms, it is done with the Vajra Medicine. For destroying all epidemics, it is done with the Vajra Elixir. For destroying untimely death, it is done with the Vajra Dūrvā Grass. For destroying all the lower realms, such as the hells, it is done with the Vajra Tearing Apart of Evil. For accomplishing every body, it is done with the Vajra Supreme Body. For accomplishing all speech, it is done with the Vajra Secret Supreme Tongue, which with one's own tongue truthfully reveals all things past, future, and present. For accomplishing every mind, it is done with the Vajra Generation of All Minds. For the accomplishment of all vajras, it is done with the Utterly Pure Vajra. For fully entering all maṇḍalas, it is done with the Vajra Wheel. For the accomplishment of binding everything with mudrās, it is done with the Vajra Secret Great Mudrā of the Samaya. For accomplishing all the samayas of everyone, it is done with the Vajra Samaya Mudrā. For accomplishing all rites, it is done with the Vajra Secret Offering. For all accomplishments, it is done with the Vajra Secret Accomplishment of Great Bliss. For every excellence, it is done with the Vajra Mudrā of Deliverance from Downfalls. For attaining the wisdom of omniscience, it is done with the Vajra Wisdom Mudrā. For making offerings to all, it is done with the Vajra Secret Dance. For the sublime accomplishment of all vajras, it is done with the Vajra of the Great Bliss Vajra. For the sublime accomplishment of all great beings, it is done with the Great Bliss of Vajrasattva. For the sublime accomplishment of all the tathāgatas, it is done with the Great Bliss of the Supreme Secret of Vajrasattva. For the sublime accomplishment of all vajra holders, it is done with the Great Bliss Vajra Secret Samaya Mudrā.

From the great king of ritual manuals, the Great Bliss Vajra Secret: the extensive rite of the offering of the Supremely Joyful Vajra Secret.

Then the Blessed One, the great Vairocana, blessed by the samaya being of the Great Bliss Vajra and possessed of the secret Dharma, holding the fist of all the tathāgatas, entered the samādhi called the Vajra That Blesses the Wheel of All the Tathāgatas, and made this request to his own Vajrasattva:

"For the full accomplishment of great bliss
And for the purification of great passion,
Please bring forth the sublime family,
The infinite tathāgatas themselves."

Then the Blessed One, the great Vajradhara, having performed the indivisible overpowering of the great bliss samaya of all the tathāgatas, having fully abided in all the vajras of the Blessed One's body, speech, and mind, and having emanated equally the very self of all, spoke this dhāraṇī of the mudrā of the Great Bliss Vajra:

oṃ dhaṃ

Then, as soon as this was spoken,
Everything shook in six ways,
And all the buddhas, together with their heirs,
Gathered from every side and became manifest.
Through the application of the great maṇḍala,
They took their seats all around for the accomplishment of all;
And with a special great laughter
They laughed out in great wonder.

Then the Blessed One, the Tathāgata Vajrasattva, likewise entered absorption in the vajra and spoke this maṇḍala of the dhāraṇī called the Great Bliss Samaya Vajra Secret:

"Having drawn it thus, according to the Dharma,
With the Victor on the left in the pose of pride,
And having set out the supreme of supreme samayas,
One says, samaya staṃ.
The tathāgatas, together with their heirs,
Should be shown holding vajras, weapons, and the like;
The bodhisattvas, the great beings,
With flowers and the like, and palms joined at the crown."

The special feature of the extensive rite of entering it and so forth is this: one should have the disciples enter with the mudrā of homage to all the tathāgatas and with this vajra song:

"Through gathering Vajrasattva,
Unsurpassed Vajraratna,
And through singing Vajradharma,
One will perform Vajrakarma."

Then, having taken one's seat, one should summon the wisdom dhāraṇī of all the tathāgatas with the Great Bliss Vajra Secret Hook:

oṃ bajradhārasuramahāsukhe sarbahārarmapamataya sarbatathāgatajānamai mantrapādaiḥ jaḥ hūṃ baṃ hoḥ

Then all the tathāgatas, together with the holders, will truly arrive. Signs such as happiness of mind will appear, and many miracles are seen. Then the extensive rite, the practice, all the accomplishments the mind desires, and the sublime accomplishment, meaningful, unobstructed, and swift, are achieved. One should do likewise.

Then, even without prior accomplishment, by reciting once one will perform the rites. For protecting everyone, it is done with the Vajra Uṣṇīṣa. For bringing all sentient beings under control, it is done with the Vajra Ūrṇā. For attaining the whole nature of reality, it is done with the Vajra Nature of Reality. The accomplishment of offering to all the tathāgatas is done with the Vajra Offering. For taming all the malevolent, it is done with the Vajra Wrathful One. For completing the conduct of awakening without remainder, it is done with the Vajra Mudrā of Conduct. For crushing all māras, it is done with the Crusher of All Māras. For becoming the very self of all the tathāgatas, it is done with the Vajra Fist of Wisdom.

From the Great Bliss Vajra Secret: the extensive rite of the great secret of all the tathāgatas. The teaching of the maṇḍala of the tathāgatas.

Then the Blessed One, the Tathāgata whose command is meaningful and unobstructed, entered the samādhi called the Vajra of the Wrathful One of Great Bliss, the Means of the Great Vajra Mind of All the Tathāgatas, and made this request to his own Vajrasattva and the others:

"For the full accomplishment of great bliss
And for the purification of great passion,
In order to crush all the malevolent,
Emanate your own family, O Vajra Holder."

Then the Blessed One Vajrasattva spoke this Supremely Joyful Vajra Wrathful One, called the Vajra Samaya of the Command of All the Tathāgatas:

hūṃ raktajvalamālāpralaya agnipratipatateja mahāsurata bajrakrodha jaḥ hūṃ baṃ hoḥ phaṭ

Then, as soon as this was spoken, from the vajra of the Blessed One Vajrapāṇi there came forth wrathful ones whose essence was red light blazing on every side, with contorted brows and gathered wrathful frowns, with tawny beards and blazing eyes, with overpowering faces, vajra fangs bared over the lower lip, with hair blazing upward, and holding vajras, bows, and the like; and they took their places in the manner of the great maṇḍala of the Blessed One. The Blessed One, for his part, having tamed all the malevolent of the three realms, blessed them with the great vajra samaya.

One should lay out the lines of the great maṇḍala,
And there first place the Wrathful One,
Reciting suratodbhava hūṃ:
Blazing with the light of a blue utpala,
The supreme one, holding the vajra in the pose of pride,
His face with fangs slightly bared,
His face laughing with wrath,
Seated in the vajra posture,
Its light the turbulent blaze of rays.
In the manner of the great maṇḍala,
Draw wrathful ones everywhere.

With the great mudrā of the Supremely Joyful Vajra Wrathful One the master should first enter, and the disciples likewise. Once the master has entered, he should summon with that same wrathful hook:

hūṃ surata bajrakroṇha ayate mahāsukha bajrasatva ja sarba bajrakula netra prabiśaya hūṃ sarba mudrāgaṇa bandhaya baṃ sarba mayā mabiśva kuru ho sarba karma netra satvaya a

Then the whole vajra family, together with the Blessed One, comes and is accomplished. For the master and the disciples, signs of delight and of the descent of the wrathful one will truly arise. Further, if even a little arises, then its accomplishment, the attainments, and the extensive rite are likewise. If it is not accomplished, one recites it once.

For victory over the entire three realms, it is done with the Vajra Mudrā of the Maker of hūṃ. For taming all the realms of sentient beings, it is done with the Vajra Secret of the Wrathful One. For complete victory over the three worlds, it is done with the Mudrā of Victory over the Three Worlds. For summoning everyone, making them enter, making them descend, binding them, bringing them under control, destroying them, paralyzing them, putting them to sleep, sealing them, overthrowing them, and killing them, it is done with the Great Bliss Vajra Wrathful One. For making everyone fall, it is done with the Vajra Subtle Wrathful One. For making everyone descend, it is done with the Vajra Wrathful One Who Brings Down. For utterly suppressing everyone, it is done with the Vajra Loud Laughter. For establishing all rites, it is done with the Great Bliss Vajra Samaya Wrathful One.

From the Great Bliss Vajra Secret: the extensive rite of the samaya of the Victorious Vajra Secret, arisen from Supreme Joy.

Then the Blessed One entered the samādhi called the Vajra Wisdom Mudrā of All the Tathāgatas, the unobscured Dharma of all the tathāgatas, meaningful, unobstructed, and wholly pure, and made this request to his own Vajrasattva:

"For the full accomplishment of great bliss
And for the purification of great passion,
O great supreme one, please bring forth
The great and pure lotus family."

Then the Blessed One, the great Vajradhara, having performed the overpowering called the Vajra That Gazes upon All Phenomena, spoke this mantra called the Great Bliss Vajra Secret Lotus:

oṃ hrī baṃ ho

As soon as this was spoken, there came forth from the mouth of the Blessed One great lotus beings, radiant with the color of the lotus, like the light of the moon's disc, holding lotus petals and the like, bearing the empowerment of the tathāgatas, with crowns of matted hair and wearing red robes, and they took their places in the manner of the great maṇḍala. The Blessed One, laughing broadly, holding a vajra in his right hand and a lotus in his left, likewise with vajra pride, sat in the vajra posture.

Then, the maṇḍala:

In the manner of the great maṇḍala
One lays out the lines of the whole maṇḍala.
One places Vajra Lokeśvara
And draws the assemblies of lotus emblems.

Then, having entered by means of the Vajra Lotus, one should summon with the two hooks of the forefingers:

oṃ hrī baṃ hoḥ oṃ hrīḥ mhāsukha bajra ālokeśvarāya me ā āhe prabeśa dhi dhi me bhaba siddhi mame ra mudrā pādaiḥ jaḥ hūṃ baṃ hoḥ

Then the Blessed One Avalokiteśvara comes and bestows every supreme gift. Signs of light will appear. All the extensive rites, the practice, and all the accomplishments are likewise. Even without prior accomplishment, by reciting it once one performs all the rites. For purifying all obscurations, it is done with the Great Lotus. For protecting everyone, it is done with the Lotus Uṣṇīṣa. For making everyone impassioned, it is done with the Gazing upon the Lotus. For attaining the samādhi of total purity, it is done with the Opening of the Lotus. For bringing all women under control, it is done with the Passion of the Great Lotus. For bringing all men under control, it is done with the Lord of the Great Lotus. For sending dreams, it is done with the Great Red Lotus. For obtaining the supreme from the Blessed One, it is done with the Vajra Secret Lotus Free of Delusion.

From the Great Bliss Lotus Secret: the extensive rite of the Great Bliss lotus family.

Then the Blessed One, the Tathāgata Īśvara, master of all the three realms, entered the samādhi called the Mudrā of the Jewel Empowerment That Fulfills All Wishes, and made this request to his own Vajrasattva:

"For the full accomplishment of great bliss
And for the purification of great passion,
Please bring forth the supreme family of the great jewel
For the sake of prosperity."

Then the Blessed One, having performed the overpowering called the Establishment of the Empowerment Samaya of All the Tathāgatas, spoke this vidyā-mantra called the Great Bliss Vajra Jewel:

oṃ litidhabhaṇṭaṭate trāṃ

Then, as soon as this was spoken, a precious jewel was drawn forth from the Great Bliss vajra of the Blessed One's empowerment and came to rest in his left hand. And that pure precious jewel became vajra jewels the color of flawless emerald, empowered by their own light, holding emblems such as jewel arrows, and they entered in the manner of the great maṇḍala of the Blessed One.

Then, the drawing of the maṇḍala:

One should lay out the lines of the whole maṇḍala,
Place there the lord Vajraratna,
And draw the great family of the jewel.

Having entered with the mudrā of Vajraratna, one should likewise summon with the vajra hook of the two forefingers:

oṃ mahāsukha bajra bibhūte raya bhagabāna taṃ ākarṣaya gira baṃ sarba tathāgata agaṃ saṃtra samabena nāma bhe ramatra bhe traṃ paḍaḍa svāhā

Then the Blessed One Ākāśagarbha comes, together with all the jewels. The sign of the hair standing on end from the crown of the head will appear, and one should understand, "The Blessed One is empowering me." Then one should have the disciples enter, and perform all the extensive rites in the same way. Then the rites that require no prior accomplishment succeed through a single recitation. For obtaining all empowerments, it is done with the Vajra Jewel Sprout. For obtaining all aims, it is done with the Tip of the Vajra Jewel Banner. For fulfilling all wishes, it is done with the Vajra Mudrā of the Emblem of the Great Jewel. For obtaining a great treasure, it is done with the Vajraratna Mudrā of What Lies Beneath. For obtaining the mudrā of vajra empowerment, it is done with the Mudrā of the Underground of the Great Vajra Jewel. For obtaining vajra empowerment, it is done with the Great Vajra Garland. For obtaining the empowerment of a great bodhisattva, it is done with the Great Vajra Jewel Garland. For obtaining the empowerment of a tathāgata, it is done with the Garland of the Sublime Empowerment of the Vajra Jewel. For obtaining empowerment as a great vajra holder, it is done with the Garland of the Vajra Jewel Crown of the Supreme Body of All the Tathāgatas.

From the Great Bliss Vajra Secret: the extensive rite of the jewel family of all bliss and happiness.

Then the Blessed One, having attained the wisdom blessed by the vajra that gathers all the families, entered the samādhi called the Vajra Mudrā of the Wheel That Brings In All Sentient Beings, and made this request to the Blessed One Vajrasattva, who is without beginning or end:

"You who are fully accomplished in great bliss,
For the purification of great passion
And for the sake of great bliss,
Please bring beings in, just as they wish."

Then the Blessed One, the great Vajradhara, victorious over all, tamer of all beings, bestower of all samayas and all accomplishments, the Teacher, the accomplisher, unobstructed in strength and in overpowering adversaries, meaningfully accomplished by teaching all in accord with them, having performed the overpowering called the Samaya Vajra Holder, spoke this heart of all, meaningful and incomparable, called the Great Bliss Samaya of All Accomplishments:

hoḥ āḥ hūṃ hrīḥ trāṃ

Then, as soon as this was spoken, all the maṇḍalas, mundane and supramundane, of all the tathāgatas and the rest, came to abide fully in that great maṇḍala and became one single maṇḍala. Then the Blessed One, the great Vajradhara, spoke the secret of the Great Bliss Vajra Samaya that brings in all sentient beings:

"In the manner of the great maṇḍala
One should draw all the maṇḍalas throughout.
The first, the great maṇḍala,
Should be visualized at its center.
On its sides one should draw
The four maṇḍalas of the families.
At the edge of the outer boundary
Draw the assemblies of the outer maṇḍala.
At all the doors, for entry,
They abide as their own mudrās.
Gathering the five sublime maṇḍalas,
One should enter by the force of the vajra."

All the extensive rites, all the accomplishments, and all the rites are likewise. The extensive rite of the gathering of the families.

Then all the samaya beings, the gods and the rest, having received their respective permissions from the Blessed One, bowed at the feet of the Blessed One, the great Vajradhara, who is meaningful bliss for all sentient beings, and offered these, their respective secret heart mantras. Then the offering of the three worlds:

oṃ bhrūṃ trāṃ

Then, the drawing of the maṇḍala:

Four-cornered and with four doors:
This is the supreme outer maṇḍala.
In its center, for the sake of accomplishment,
Draw the one who bears a bow and a khaṭvāṅga,
Holding an arrow and a cup:
Madhukara and Jayakara,
The accomplisher of every practice.
With the mudrā of the trident emblem,
Kneeling with one knee raised,
Having entered and bound the samaya,
One should perform the true summoning:

oṃ bhūr bhuva svāhā

By reciting it twenty-one times,
One is encircled entirely by all.
To those who have come for the sake of all accomplishments
Give whatever torma one has found.
The master, by means of the entering,
Should have all the disciples enter.
One should declare the reality of the supreme samaya
And the reality of the secret:
"If you speak of it to one who has not been examined,
Or if you do not fully practice here
The Dharma just as it has been taught,
The trident will smash your heart."
Sattva, rajas, and tamas,
Brahmā, Viṣṇu, and Maheśvara (Śiva):
These three names
Should never be held in contempt.
Whoever has held them in contempt
Should enter again and again
And perform the worship of the maṇḍala.
For the attainment of all accomplishments,
Having also displayed the mudrās,
He should make the offering of dance;
With the five qualities of sense pleasure
He should always worship the maṇḍala.

Then the secret goddesses offered their own mudrās.

hrīḥ hūṃ śrī bhyo

As for the making of all the maṇḍalas:
It is square, with four gates,
Properly furnished with four lines,
Together with four great pillars,
And beautified by four corner pieces.
Likewise, in its center
The four maṇḍalas should be drawn.
At the gates the four who face outward
Should be placed together with their heart mantras,
Joyful, holding weapons in their hands,
With the proud bearing of those who bring accomplishment.
Then, first, into the maṇḍala
One should likewise enter completely.
The sisters, with their own mudrās,
Should enter in their proper sequence.
Having entered, with incense and so forth
Having made offerings according to the rite,
Through the full accomplishment of that very secret
The summoning is properly performed.

oṃ kālimaniradeprotalamebhūrākāsiparastāmayahebahoba barhebidyetīmayasvāhā siddhikesiddhistāmaahe

This is done with these mudrās. hrīḥ hūṃ śrī bhyo Then all will descend. When they have descended, they should be made to enter. Then, in the same way, having taught the binding of the mudrās and having proclaimed the oath, one should speak the secret:

"Those who abide for the benefit of beings,
Those who teach the maṇḍala wherever they are:
If you do not guard the secret,
You will have killed them.
Attachment, aversion, and delusion,
And likewise food, drink, and taste,
Through Dharma give happiness to the world,
And through what is not Dharma give suffering.
With the mudrās and the offering of dance
One should make offerings as one wishes.
As much as there is, and just as one desires,
One should rely upon the objects of the senses."

Then all the asuras offered their own heart mantra:

oṃ sarba mahācakro prabartaya darśaya baṃ hūṃ phaṭ

A four-cornered maṇḍala,
Having been dug to the depth of about a cubit,
Should have its gates drawn facing upward.
Saṃvara and the others should be drawn there.
Having fully entered the wheel,
One should summon with the proper summoning.
With offerings of flowers and so forth,
And with meat and so forth, one should make the torma.

oṃ bilcucakra ānaya śamabarci macita traṃ sura adhipan heśo haṃ sarba māyā ghṛtaṃ dhapame siddhya hā hā svāhā

Then every illusion the mind desires is accomplished. The bringing in of disciples should also be done in the same way.

Then the four families of nāgas offered their heart mantra: phuḥ

Then, as for their maṇḍala:
It is four-cornered, with four gates,
Drawn all around with lines of snakes,
Encircled by the tips of nāga hoods,
And at the corners the tips of their tails are knotted.
In its center the bearer of the earth,
The nāga king Śeṣa, should be drawn.
All the families of the great nāgas,
Those endowed with wisdom, should be drawn.
Then, through the force of meditative absorption,
Wrathful and running, one should enter.
Having fully entered that maṇḍala,
One should summon with the mudrā of the hood.

oṃ hrīḥ anīla jaba bega pṛthvī bhinad dhar śeṣaṇa āya hrīḥ nuya nāga kula anaya hūṃ phuḥ samaya prabeśaya phuḥ sarbaṃ sādhaya phuḥ phuḥ phuḥ phuḥ

Then all the nāgas, repeating "phuḥ," enter the maṇḍala and descend, and they are won. Then the master should render everything free of poison with the mudrā of the drawn image. From then on, even if one eats anything at all, it is digested like nectar. By dancing together with these, one becomes able to burn, destroy, and devour all poisons. Then one should give the pledge as one wishes:

"Attachment, aversion, and delusion:
These are the three poisons of the world.
If one has passed beyond the truth,
This world of the living endures forever.
If you wish to obtain accomplishments,
Do not treat lightning with contempt,
Do not turn back hail,
And do not kill any snake whatsoever."

This concludes the extensive rite of the vajra secret of the great pledge of Great Bliss and so forth, from the great king of ritual manuals, the Vajra Secret of Great Bliss. The second section: from the root tantra, the ritual of the ten maṇḍalas.

Then, on the summit of Mount Meru,
The mountain of the great vajra,
Beautiful with stainless splendor
From the clear light of vajra jewels,
Four-cornered and with four gates,
Beautified by its gateway arches,
Worshiped by hosts of vajra goddesses
Who dwell upon its parapets,
Beautified by vajra jewels
Set upon half-moons,
Resounding with the sound of banners and bells
On silken streamers stirred by the wind,
Adorned with nets and mirrors hung there
And with great garlands of flowers,
Is Great Bliss, the great vajra,
Brought together by all the vajras,
The great abode of Vajrasattva,
Taught with certainty by all the buddhas,
The supreme abode of the bodhisattvas,
The abode of all who hold the vajra.
Great Bliss, the great vajra,
Called the great supreme jewel:
Within the great vajra mansion
Great Bliss dwells.
The being without beginning or end
Is Vajrasattva, the great joy,
Samantabhadra, the self of all,
Lord of lords, in the manner of vajra pride.
Whatever is the mind of all beings
Is explained as "sattva" because it is firm;
Through meditative equipoise it is concentration;
Because it is certain, it becomes vajra.
Through applying the blessing of the sattva,
It becomes Vajrasattva once again.
That very one is the permanent Blessed One,
Who follows the conduct of awakening.
When one discerns one's own mind and so forth,
In accordance with the rite of the buddhas' awakening,
Māra is the buddha, the great vajra;
He is that very great Vajradhara.
He is the Tathāgata family;
He is the great vajra family;
He is the pure lotus family;
He is the holy jewel family.
He is desire and liberation,
Of attachment and of freedom from attachment.
The three times, the three existences, the three supreme ones,
The three worlds and the three realms:
That supreme one is all that is unmoving,
Firm, the essence, the great jewel;
And that which moves, the mind's formations, the means:
He is all, the doer of all.
The first being, the great being,
The bodhisattva Supreme Bliss and the others,
Having sat in the cross-legged posture of the sattva,
Have entered the center of the lotus,
Surrounded by the various vajra goddesses
Together with a host of queens.
For the full accomplishment of great bliss,
For the purification of great attachment,
For the sake of all those to be trained without exception,
And for the full accomplishment of all accomplishments,
The great beings, oneself and the others,
For the full accomplishment of all accomplishments,
The pledges of the supreme pledge
Are guarded even by the Victors.
Brandishing the vajra with pride,
Truly holding vajra pride,
For the purity of all the mudrās
And for the supreme accomplishment of Vajrasattva,
Great Bliss, the great vajra,
In accomplishing all accomplishments,
Is truly endowed with the wisdom mudrā:
This is the holy great heart mantra.
Through it even those who lack merit
Have likewise entered every maṇḍala without exception,
And all the accomplishments of the supreme,
Unfailing and unimpeded, are not hard to find.
Great Bliss, the great vajra,
Supreme among secrets, the firm pledge,
Accomplishes all accomplishments
And swiftly accomplishes all rites.
Without beginning or end, arisen first,
Lofty above all merits,
The Saṃvara of the radiant ones,
And for the purification of great attachment,
Called "the meditation of passion,"
Wisdom just as it is fully enters.
The heart mantra together with the near-heart mantra
The Blessed One spoke in the first eon:
hūṃ
Then, merely through speaking this,
All the buddhas together with their heirs
Gathered closely together
And brought forth all beings.
Having brought forth all the various beings,
The Lord asked about accomplishment:
"How is it accomplished here in an instant?
Great being, tell us quickly!"
Then, as for the first maṇḍala:
Having first blessed the maṇḍala,
Having accomplished according to the rite
The pledge of the vajra supreme over all,
From then on beings arise
Who follow their karma just as it is,
Who always engage in every evil,
Who cling to what is bad, and who are lazy.
Having seen these three kinds of people,
The lords of the world asked:
"Blessed One, how, then,
Can the childish bring forth virtue in every way?"
Having heard their words,
He spoke these words:
The Vajra Holder, maker of all,
Looked upon those who die through their karma.
Having heard those words of his,
Vajrasattva, the Vajra Holder,
And all the buddhas together with their heirs
Spoke thus with great compassion:
"For the full accomplishment of great bliss
And for the purification of intense attachment,
Please explain that heart mantra
Of the extensive rite supreme over all."
Then the Teacher, the Vajra Holder,
Is the bestower of all accomplishments,
The great bliss of all rites,
The supreme first vajra secret;
And further, the teaching of the heart mantra
And the great rites of the maṇḍala and so forth,
Together with the various later tantras,
Fully accomplish all accomplishments.
All the worlds, the great realms,
Equal in number to the finest particles,
Through this very great heart mantra
Have come to the holy bliss of accomplishment.
Then those Sugatas,
Having heard his supreme words,
At that time made supplication to him
Who bestows the accomplishment of the first maṇḍala:
"To train those free of attachment,
Should freedom from attachment be taught?
Or, for the purification of all attachment,
Speak the rite of your own heart."

Then the Blessed One, singing the great vajra song, laid out the maṇḍala:

"Mind of holy bliss, passionate toward all,
Vajrasattva, you have come forth;
Supremely joyful, the great bliss of all beings,
Firm and exalted one, today turn toward me with swift accomplishment!"

Thus singing the song of the maṇḍala, he laid it out.

Through the application of the great maṇḍala
The first maṇḍala should be laid out.
In every part belonging to Vajrapāṇi,
The host of vajra goddesses and the rest,
Exactly according to the rite,
Should be adorned and made to enter.
On the left, the maiden who holds the vajra
Should be embraced, and the yoga maintained.
With the supreme mudrā of pride
Together with the vajra, one enters oneself.
Having entered that maṇḍala,
One should circumambulate three times.
Seated before Vajrasattva,
One should also speak that very secret:

hūṃ hūṃ mahāsukha bajra satva jaḥ hūṃ baṃ hoḥ smaya stvaṃ anurakto me bhaba sutoṣyo me bhaba driḍho me bhaba supoṣyo me bhaba adhitiṣṭa bhagabā banadinidhena svāhā mahāsukha bajra sastva samaya guhya samaya siddhi mame prayaccha oṃ oṃ ha ha ha ha a

Then, rejoicing greatly, one should speak words of delight to the vajra goddess.

oṃ bajra driṣṭi samaya kemaṭa bajra kili kili ho hūṃ smara bajriṇe banadha hūṃ hūṃ tuṣṭa bajraṇṛ hūṃ

Then, sitting close before the Blessed One, one should make this request to the Blessed One: "In the same way, Blessed One, grant me accomplishment!"

"Swiftly, toward passion for all,
Through kili kili all things are possible.
Recalling passion for all,
May I always be well pleased."

oṃ mahāsukha siddhi bajra satva jaḥ hūṃ baṃ hoḥ siddhaṃ samaya stvaṃ siddhi hūṃ

Then, having been empowered with the vajra water of Great Bliss and having drunk it, one should hold the five-pronged vajra and state one's own name, saying, "I am Vajrasattva." As before, one should make offerings with the offering of dance. Having made offerings together with the disciple, with whatever objects of the senses are at hand, one should come out. The disciple should be brought in, and when one also enters there oneself, one has entered all the maṇḍalas.

Since one performs the activity of the wisdom of all vajra masters, who are lords of all mudrās, who know all the pledges, who are able to subdue all and to benefit all, and who are perfect in all accomplishments, by the mere fact of one's own entering, even one who is not a master and has not entered the maṇḍala is blessed by the Blessed One in a single instant. Why is that? Because the Blessed One himself, having certainly become the vajra master through his own pledge, brings about the entry. He also performs the extensive rite of all activities. Through the blessing of this heart mantra, even if one has not entered the maṇḍala, one has entered all maṇḍalas and becomes the master of all. By reciting it a single time one cannot be slain and cannot be overcome, and everywhere, through the various letters, there is seeing, hearing, awareness, and knowing. Having brought forth all the tathāgatas without exception, together with the great Vajradhara, they bestow all accomplishments upon that person. It is not a matter of purification and of reliance. Even those who make obstacles will not inflict suffering. The Blessed One, Great Bliss, Vajradhara, who is supremely firm, supremely delighted, passionate, and supremely expansive, swiftly brings about all accomplishments, all perfections, all wisdoms, and all activities, with an accomplishment that is beyond doubt.

Moreover, whoever writes it, or has it written, or recites it a single time, or retains it, even without practicing it and without entering the maṇḍala, will in this very life accomplish all the supreme accomplishments, just as he wishes. Why is that? It is like this: the Unfailing and Unimpeded, the lord of the lords of all the tathāgatas, cannot be obstructed even by all the tathāgatas, because he is passion for all. Even the awakening of the buddhas arises from passion. Passion comes from relying on desire. If one does not desire it, how could one come to all the states such as supreme great bliss? Thus spoke the unimpeded Blessed One, the great bliss of great passion.

"When it is accomplished, great bliss
Is bestowed in an instant through vajra passion.
This is the pledge of practice;
It is hard for anyone to transgress.
One who commits great evil,
Or likewise one who has not seen the maṇḍala,
Having purified that evil by a single recitation,
Becomes accomplished through accomplishing the supreme.
If, as taught in the ritual manual or as one wishes,
One meditates, recollecting the image,
Or the heart mantra, or a book,
Because it is everything, the lord is accomplished.
And for one who has no image,
And even if there is no book,
One should write the syllable hūṃ everywhere
And practice the accomplishment of oneself."

Thus spoke the great Vajradhara, possessed of unfailing and unimpeded accomplishment.

Then, when one wishes to accomplish
All the activities of this king of ritual manuals,
Having laid out the lines of all the maṇḍalas,
One should draw the mudrās that are emblems.

Having entered with the first vajra, one should declare:

"From great passion comes great unimpededness;
From great unimpededness, great bliss;
From great bliss the vajra is shown;
From the vajra the mudrā arises.
Through fully joining the image
Of the vajra of body, speech, and mind,
All the mudrās are explained;
This pledge is hard to transgress.
When, by the mere binding of a mudrā,
The buddhas, Vajradhara and the rest,
Are truly gathered together,
The world of sameness is accomplished.
Just as the command
Of a king's seal is hard to transgress,
So too even all the Victors
Are unable to transgress the mudrā.
Buddhahood itself, or Vajradhara,
Not attained even in ten million eons,
How is it attained in this very life
By gathering the supreme mudrā!
Just as the world to be trained is,
So accomplishment has been taught.
Its means is the mudrā,
Supreme in bringing about all accomplishments.
Likewise, through the extensive rite,
All accomplishments are beyond doubt."

Then this is the binding of the mudrās of the secret pledge of the first maṇḍala:

Joining all the fingers together
Is called the vajra palms.
From binding the fingers inward
The vajra binding is properly explained.
Within the vajra binding
The index fingers and thumbs are properly joined.
Raising the right thumb,
Inserting it and pressing, one holds the hook.
Having bound the five gathered arrows,
It rests within the binding of the middle fingers.
With the thumbs made even, the tip a vajra:
This is the first holy bliss, the firm one.
The tips of thumb and index joined, and the tips of the middle fingers
Joined like eyeballs, are held.
Beneath the vajra of the middle fingers
One should press down and squeeze with the thumbs.
Like the vajra guide,
The ring fingers and little fingers are opened;
Circling them and placing them upon the knee
Arises from the first vajra.
Scattering upward and pouring downward,
Joining the thumbs and circling,
Stretching out fully and anointing,
Circling and resting upon the crown,
Circling and placing at the heart,
Circling all around and laughing;
Coming forth upward from the mouth,
And likewise circling with dance;
With the two index fingers,
The left index finger held by the right;
Binding the index finger and the thumb,
And placing the thumb within the last finger:
These are the mudrās of the fist.

Then, the Unfailing and Unimpeded: the binding of the karma mudrās belonging to these alone, which is supremely secret and unsurpassed and which accomplishes the whole range of the highest activities, cannot be overpowered even by me together with all the tathāgatas.

Here, from the central vajra of Great Bliss come placing the tips of the vajra nails at the lips and so forth, fully turning the wheel, and the vajra jewel. Of these, which bring about every accomplishment as one wishes, the accomplishments are those of the great Vajradhara, of the buddhas and bodhisattvas, of the vajra jewel, and the supreme accomplishment.

With the Vajra Guide comes the activity of the eyeballs. By looking with the right and the left eye, there is passion for all. By drawing the eyeballs inward, there is summoning. By making them enter, all are made to enter. By pressing, there is binding. By not closing them, there is subjugation.

With Vajrakilikilā: when the middle fingers are pinched, and by embracing, pressing, playing, and gently striking the middle fingers and the tips of the thumbs, through seeing, playing, and pressing one plays with all, and women in divine form appear everywhere, among the moving and the unmoving. For as long as one plays with them, one also plays with the consort of Vajrasattva.

With Vajramakarī: by pressing, raising up, tasting, going near, and eating swiftly, one binds with steadfast passion, is victorious over all, dwells within water, walks upon it, and accomplishes the eating of everything.

With the vajra circling: by moving and moving, loosening, pressing, drawing the middle fingers together toward each other, and swiftly tearing apart the vajra of the middle fingers, all the wrathful ones blaze, and full pacification, the sealing of all, causing to fall, and killing are swiftly accomplished.

If one makes offerings with the offerings in which the thumbs enter within the vajra, one will be honored even by the great Vajradhara. Likewise, by the activities of the goddesses in which the thumbs enter within the vajra, all the daughters of the gods are summoned and engaged in every activity, such as play. By the activities of the inner fist of Vajrāṅkuśī and the others come all the accomplishments that the mind desires.

Then, the tantra of all accomplishments: through the great mudrā of Vajrasattva, the accomplishments of accomplishment, of pledge, of wisdom, and of activity are accomplished. Accomplishment through the Unfailing: swiftly, through the blessing of the Blessed One, the self of all, what the mind desires is accomplished. With the vajra of supreme joy, from the moving of the tip of the sign, everything comes; and likewise from the moving of the womb. Through performing the activity of the central vajra of Great Bliss come all accomplishments. If, with the Graceful One, keeping the vajra eye of joy unmoving, one practices all activities, there will be unimpeded accomplishment. Likewise, through their own hand emblems, all accomplishments will be accomplished.

First, once every day,
One should recite the heart mantra of the great mudrā.
With the vajra near-heart mantra
One should definitely apply all the mudrās.
Everything is accomplished unfailingly.
Great Bliss, the great vajra,
The supreme secret rite, the later one,
The unfailing pledge of practice,
Bestows accomplishment in an instant through the heart mantra.
The root tantra, together with the later tantra,
The tantra of mudrās, is unsurpassed.
Likewise the supreme accomplishment
Is the accomplishment of unsurpassed wisdom.

This completes the extensive rite of the tantra of all the mudrās of the first maṇḍala and the rest.

Then, the tantra of the great mudrā of the Blessed One:

Placing it on the left lap,
One should set the lotus feet comfortably.
Having sat in the vajra cross-legged posture,
Holding the body upright and at ease,
Brandishing the vajra with pride,
Truly holding vajra pride,
Through the vajra of body, speech, and mind
One becomes Vajrasattva oneself.
Through this, Vajrasattva himself,
Buddhahood, or being an heir of the buddhas,
And lordship over all accomplishments
Will be accomplished in this very life.
Through the vajra of body, speech, and mind,
Having followed the mudrā just as it is,
One becomes oneself the great being
Endowed with the mudrā of the emblem.

This is the great mudrā.

Then, the binding of the pledge mudrās: the vajra of Great Bliss is explained. The four, the vajra arrow and the others, arise from it. The two index fingers form a hook turned outward. Then embracing with the thumbs. With the two thumbs opened, the two index fingers draw out the tips of the middle fingers, the ring fingers and little fingers are joined evenly, and their tips are opened apart: this is raising the makara banner. Then circling at the two thighs and remaining there. Likewise, at the corners, by circling the vajra palms. The mudrās of the gates are made with the fist of vajra pride.

The great mudrā of supreme great bliss
Likewise fully bestows accomplishment;
The various pledges, the arrow and the rest,
Bestow the supreme great accomplishment.

Then, the Dharma mudrās: ho bid hūṃ dhṛk hūṃ. These too bestow all accomplishments.

Then, the karma mudrās are to be understood through vajra fists without the vajra. With the left hand in vajra pride, the right fist is held at the heart in a raised manner: this is Vajragarvā, which makes supreme joy firm and bestows all accomplishments. Raising the bow and arrow is the Vajra Weapon Goddess. Below is embracing. Then raising the banner. Then raising the two prides. With grace, releasing with the last fingers and so on, circling fully to the right in a dance like flashing lightning, one brings down from the crown of the head the cupped palms holding flowers and the rest. Releasing the vajra palms, likewise with grace, one should cast down the offering. The vajra dance is at the inner corners.

Whether complete or incomplete,
Or else just as they are obtained,
Through the ladies of the flower offering and the rest
The supreme accomplishment is bestowed, and the common.

This is the full grasp of the signs of the application of the pledge mudrās. These too are practices of unfailing activity. With the vajra fist one should recite over the nectar water.

The explanation of the mudrās of the rite of entry. Of what is called "the secret pledge blessed by the vajra master of all the great maṇḍalas," the second mudrā of entry:

Having bound the vajra binding,
Into the womb of the left thumb and index finger
One inserts the sign of the right thumb;
When it is pressed, it bestows bliss.
With that bound hook of the index fingers
One should enter the supreme maṇḍala.
Or else, whatever being one enters,
That will swiftly be accomplished there.
This is the Vajra Lady of Supreme Joy.

Having taken up the vajra nectar water of the vajra with one's own fist, if with that stream of water one confers empowerment and has the disciple drink, he becomes empowered. Through all the empowerments he will be in the prime of youth, free of illness, and endowed with all accomplishments. Fevers, grahas, poisons, compounded poisons, ḍākinīs, mudrās and the like will be unable to harm him. In the cross-legged posture one should offer the dance and the offerings. Then the offering is made with the vajra dance. The offering of the vajra dance, made by singing the vajra song, is done with the dance of the universal monarch. Fully holding the vajra fist, with vajra praise one should raise the vajra weapon and the rest; and at the end one pays homage with the face and moving eyes, together with the secret laughter of the Graceful One, the vajra of joy. The Blessed One Vajradhara, being pleased, bestows the accomplishments of all bliss and all mental happiness, such as every joy.

Pressing the vajra binding, if one releases the bliss at one's own heart, then in releasing the maṇḍala, if through the meditation of Vajrasattva one raises up the line (rekhā) and the vajra with the mind and gradually enters the inner maṇḍala, the pledge will not be broken. If through the meditation of Vajrasattva one turns toward the maṇḍala, the pledge will not be broken.

Then the tantra of the karma mudrās. When the Great Bliss vajra is placed face down at the crown, at the forehead, and at the heart, a blazing fence arises in all directions, which even the great vajra wrathful kings cannot enter. Meditating at the heart, together with the vajra. Facing upward, one should drink. Having fully cleansed one's own face, one should release it at the face. The central vajra should be bent. One should place it at the two shoulders and place it at the heart. The empowerment of silken streamers is seen. From the womb comes the drawing forth of activity and of life. One should place it at the crown. One should touch the ground. This is with the hook of the sign of the vajra of supreme joy. With the upper tips, the womb released, one should rub and strike the palm of the left hand. One should press it. One should close the eyes. Circling and pressing, one should look with a wrathful gaze. Having circled fully, showing the fangs at the corners of the mouth, the threatening forefinger is made with the right index finger. One should rise up on the index finger. One should press the thumb. With the syllable mu the mudrā is released.

First, one should strike with the vajra of the little fingers. That same one with a transformed face. That same one, circled fully, rests at the forehead facing upward. With that same one, one strikes the sky. With moving and closed eyes, the bound mudrā is released. One should touch with the jewel of the little fingers. With the index fingers even, one should exchange the toes, and eat, or place it with the mind. With the index finger, one performs the fire offering. The vajra of supreme joy should be released at the face. One performs the fire offering. Joining the two little fingers at the womb, one should tear the mudrā apart. Joining the ring fingers and the last fingers evenly, one should raise the fingers like a banner. Perfecting it with the vajra binding, the index fingers and thumbs opened evenly and bent, one should circle and tear it apart. With the vajra binding fully stretched out, one should touch the ground with the palms. The first vajra produces clouds. With the index finger one should bring forth rain. With the index finger one should turn it back. With the vajra gaze one should seize it away.

The vajra of supreme joy is explained. The jewel of a single index finger. The little finger is the vajra threatening forefinger. With the index finger, one draws up a spring from beneath the earth and shows it. Holding it with the Great Bliss vajra, one should move it. With the two index fingers one shows the form of a serpent's hood. With the light rays of the four tips one shows sparks of fire. With the index finger one produces wind. With the vajra of supreme joy, with the tip of the index finger, one should make the mirror, the play, the vajra gaze into the sky, and circling above. The vajra radiance should be placed at the base of all trees, upon forests, pleasure groves, and all grains. With a sharp index finger one should recite. By oneself one should lead all aright. One should circle the outer army in front. With the two vajra fists one should circle like flashing lightning. Like flashing lightning one should circle in the sky. With the right one should strike the left. The two vajra fists, moving, should be placed upon that which is unmoving. The circle of the even index fingers is the jewel. The right index finger should be inserted into the opening of the left thumb of the index-finger vajra. With the vajra of supreme joy, above in the sky there is the stirring of joy, the moving of the womb, and the stirring and binding of the central vajra. Thus are explained the Vajra Born from Mind, Vajrakilikilā, Vajramakarī, and Vajragarvā.

Making supplication with the index fingers of the vajra binding, one should cover one's own eyes with the two index fingers and meditate with the mind on whatever one wishes. One performs the fire offering. With the index fingers one should close one's own eyes. One should hold it with the teeth. One should drink. Moving the tips, one should perform the fire offering. Stretching it out fully beneath the thumb, one should give.

The supreme mudrā of the vajra body: the thumbs in the cross-legged posture of the sattva and the index fingers as a vajra: this is the supreme vajra body. With the even tops of the middle fingers forming a crest, speech is brought forth. Placing it at the heart, one should meditate on oṃ bodhicitta utapādayāmi. The sign of the thumb should be inserted into the opening of the left thumb. The two vajra fists circled like flashing lightning: this is the vajra wheel. Vajragarvā is together with the vajra. With the syllable hūṃ: the threatening forefinger. This is with the dance of the vajra of supreme joy.

Vajragarvā has been explained. With the little fingers knotted at their corners, the even tips are circled all around. Raised from below, with the tips of the index fingers even, if it is placed at the site of empowerment, it is the vajra ūrṇā. The fist of all the tathāgatas will be explained below. This is the explanation of the vajra dance. With the index finger turned outward and raised, the vajra at the heart, saying vajro ta haṃ: this is the great mudrā. Likewise, meditating on a vajra at the heart, one says mahāsaha haṃ. With the wisdom fist, tathāgato'haṃ: the great mudrā of the Blessed One has been explained.

Next, the binding of the vajra fist:

Bind the fingers in the vajra bond,
Adorned with the two bent index fingers;
Within the last two fingers
The two thumbs are joined at their tips.
The great pledge, the supreme vajra,
The fist that grants every supreme accomplishment:
Thus is the vajra fist fully set forth.

Next, the extensive rite of the tantra of mudrās of the Tathāgata family. The great mudrās are to be bound with tathāgato'haṃ. The supreme pledge rite is the thumbs and little fingers held even. The vajra is the crossed vajra. The binding of the arrow and the rest is likewise. The karma mudrās are just those. With the tathāgata fist they are bound in the same way. It is as with Aṅkuśī.
