Namely: for completely destroying all illnesses, sorrows, harms, harm-doers, all poisons, compounded poisons, executioners, distracters, vetālas, mantras, potions, obstructors, vināyakas, evil, bad dreams, untimely death, fevers, grahas, ḍākinīs, calamities, contagious diseases, faults, afflictions, and all sufferings of body, speech and mind, it is by the remover of suffering of all the tathāgatas.

For increasing life, youth, form, complexion, great splendor, radiance, strength, vigor, diligence, glory, good fortune, merit, name, renown, fame, mindfulness, intelligence, steadfastness, realization, power, samādhi, the aggregates, the formations, and body, speech and mind, it is by the great bliss of all the tathāgatas.

For accomplishing the generosity, moral discipline, patience, diligence, meditative concentration, wisdom, primordial wisdom, powers, skillful means, vajra, practice, dhāraṇīs, grounds, conduct, the discriminating knowledges, the unshared qualities of a buddha, Dharma, action, manifold deeds, the benefit of beings, the aim of the buddhas' deeds, and the body, speech, mind and great vajra of all the tathāgatas, it is by all the accomplishments of all the tathāgatas.

For accomplishing the generation of the mind set on awakening by all the tathāgatas, the perfecting of the conduct of the rite of Samantabhadra, the benefit of all beings, the demonstration of the descent, of the place of birth, and of youthful play, the renunciation, the demonstration of austerities, the going to the seat of awakening, the taming of all māras, the full awakening into great awakening, the turning of the wheel of Dharma, the crushing of all non-Buddhists (tīrthikas), the Victory over the Three Worlds, the descent from the gods, the blessing of the great assembly, the taming of all the vicious and establishing them in discipline, the rescue from all the lower realms, the teaching of all the sūtras, the blessing of all rites, the bestowal of all accomplishments, and the magical powers and miracles focused on the body, speech and mind of all the tathāgatas, it is by the manifold vajra of all the tathāgatas.

Sublime awakening, as it is relied upon
Through these mudrās in the mind of awakening,
Is not attained in that way
Through the conduct of awakening and the like.
Accomplishing the vast activities
Of unfailing and sublime merit,
It accomplishes the rites not yet accomplished,
And fully bestows the qualities of a buddha.
It should be given to all beings
Endowed with every aspect.
Even those who have not entered the maṇḍalas
It joins to it in a single instant.

This is the extensive vajra chapter on the blessing of the vajra of awakening of all the tathāgatas, from the great king of rites, Śrī Paramādya.

Then the one of great and manifold activity,
The all-pervading lord, the great Vajra Holder,
Supreme tamer of all the vicious,
Gave teaching suited to all beings.
Though he had brought forth the family of the tathāgatas,
Because the vicious, through their misdeeds,
Act according to their own minds,
The aim of all beings, their taming,
Cannot be fully accomplished by peaceful means.
All these beings
Are those whom all the buddhas must accomplish.
When the sugatas do not engage
In working the sublime benefit of beings,
Then the Lord, the very substance of all,
Made supplication to the Vajra;
He made supplication to the Blessed One,
Who tames all beings:
uttiṣṭha samaya krodhani sumbha jvāla vajra hūṃ
Then, by the mere utterance of this mantra,
Vajrapāṇi himself, the chief,
Came forth again from the vajra
Of body, speech and mind, and stood in front.
In the form of the great king of wrath
He sat well in the posture of the hero,
Bright with a garland of blazing light,
Like a second eon of burning.
With his left hand in the gesture of great vajra pride,
Brandishing fiercely, he set all in motion;
The blazing vajra blazed mightily,
As if burning all with its radiance.
The great blaze that slays
Was like a mass of the fire at the end of the eon.
Frowning with the first vajra,
The holder of the supreme vajra spoke.

Here this is the heart mantra called "the great vajra blazing like fire and the sun": hūṃ

Then, by the mere utterance of this,
The firewood of all evils was consumed.
The three worlds of the world-systems
All quaked and trembled.
The gods, the Great God (Śiva) and the rest,
Became motionless and stupefied.
All the turbulence of the blazing vajra
Likewise became like destruction itself.
The śrāvakas and the pratyekabuddhas
Drew near to nirvāṇa.
All the three existences, of gods and the rest,
Were at that time utterly stupefied.
The assembly of all the buddhas
Taught once more the awakening of the buddhas,
And the great and sublime method
Of all the beings was comprehended.
Then the supreme blazing vajra,
With turbulent light in garlands of flame,
His face with bared vajra fangs,
His countenance wrathful,
Sat well with the left leg extended,
His eyes blazing and terrifying,
His hair streaming upward and waving,
Coiling all around and blazing greatly.
The great yogin of the vajra secret
Sent forth those with bodies of the hosts of ḍākinīs,
Those who go everywhere,
And everywhere blazing turmoil arose.
Like the fire of the eon stirred by the wind,
The garlands of flame churned everywhere.
In all the world-systems without exception,
He duly exhorted all the buddhas
With their heirs, abiding there,
And encircled all the worlds.
Having summoned them, for the benefit of all beings
And to accomplish all the teachings,
He rendered those lords of the three worlds,
And all that moves and does not move, lifeless.
Then the king, the Vajra Holder,
The All-Supreme, lord of the earth,
Out of compassion for them,
Firmly spoke the revival of the dead:
oṃ uttiṣṭha mahā adhipati vajriṇi śīghraṃ utathāpaya hūṃ
This is the raising of all that moves and does not move.
Then, in the raising of the three worlds,
The dead were revived everywhere.
They were tamed by the hero Buddha,
And then tamed further by the Vajra Bearer.
Then the wrathful Vajra Holder,
Brandishing the blazing vajra,
Saying, "Abide in my pledge!"
Gave his command to the manifold beings.
They asked, "Who is this?
The Blessed One is peaceful;
Please tell us who this one in the form of a yakṣa is.
What kind of pledge do you require?
We ask you; speak in words of truth."
The Blessed One said,
"This is the Buddha, fully liberated,
The primordially born great vajra yakṣa.
To the Three Jewels, and likewise
To me, go for refuge!"
Having heard this, the three worlds,
With Gaṇapati and the lords of the hosts,
With blazing weapons like the fire-mass of the eon,
Set out to fight.
They said,
"We do not know
A buddha family or a vajra yakṣa.
We are the sole lords of the three worlds;
Why should we do what a yakṣa commands?"
Then the Vajra Holder, enraged,
Remained with a frown gathered on his brow.
Then all the frowning wrathful ones
Followed after the Vajra Holder.
With his left hand in the gesture of pride, forming the seal,
His brows and eyes drawn together, laughing,
With a voice pervading space,
Fiercely wrathful, he spoke:

Then the near-heart mantra of Trailokyavijaya, victorious over all beings without exception, is this: hi.

Then, when this heart mantra had been brought forth,
The eleven were bound, surely conquered,
And made to hold firmly to the commitment.
Empowering them with vajra names,
Vajrapāṇi made them supreme holders.
Having given them vajra names,
He set them in the samādhi of the three worlds.
Then the supreme Great Vajra,
The Protector, made this request:
"As guidance for all beings,
Please explain this ritual."
The Vajra Holder spoke:
"Outside the great maṇḍala
Properly draw a blazing wheel.
In its center should be placed
A beautiful, blue, blazing form,
His fangs slightly bared,
His face laughing with wrath,
Seated well in the posture of a sattva,
Proud and fiercely blazing,
Amid a turbulent light of blazing rays,
Intent on brandishing the vajra.
All around his figure
Are the forms of Vajrayoginīs,
Holding the weapons: vajra weapons, great spears,
Swords and nooses;
In the intermediate directions, holding arrows and khaṭvāṅgas,
Wheels and various banners.
The manifold ones of the vajra family
Are encircled by a blazing wheel.
The maṇḍala is to be laid out
Properly with seven lines:
First, the lord of the vajra mudrā;
Second, the mantra goddesses;
In the third, the vajra king,
Properly surrounded by vajra goddesses,
The great vajra lady and the great Graceful One;
In the fourth, the female singers and the rest;
The wrathful ones of the kṣatriyas, attendants and servants
Are in the fifth;
In the sixth, the supreme vajra messengers,
Yakṣas, rākṣasas and kiṃkaras,
The excellent Īśvara, Rudra and the rest,
Who tame the great fierce ones.
In the shapes of the outer maṇḍala,
In brief, the three brothers and the rest,
All the hosts of gods and others,
Should be well drawn or placed.
In the intermediate directions, those taught as goddesses;
At the center of their gates,
The wrathful ones, Vajra Aṅkuśī and the rest.
In the manner of the great maṇḍala,
With the sequence just as it should be,
Perform the extensive rite just as it is prescribed.
Wrathfully brandishing the vajra,
Bow with a wrathful frown and a smile.
Then the great master, wrathful,
Should clearly entreat the supreme Vajra Bearer.
With the threatening finger of Vajrahūṃkāra
One should fully apply the Hūṃkāra gesture,
And, with pride, seal it.
Likewise, in taming through wrath,
The great Vajradhara, delighted,
Rejoices greatly in the very wicked.
Then, taking up a flower,
Wrathfully scatter it on the great lord.
With the blazing vajra hook
One should properly summon.

oṃ mahāvajrajvālayānalārkaya hi jaḥ sarvavajrakula praveśaya hūṃ sarvamudrāṃ ganā bandhana trāṭa sarva samayaṃ sādhaya phaṭ sarva duṣṭana daha paca bhasmviṃ kuru mahākrodha akani vajreṇa hūṃ phaṭ a a a a

Then the Blessed One, the king of the wrathful ones,
The great steadfast one, will come.
All of them, the vajra family and the rest,
Will bestow all accomplishments.
Then the signs of sounds
Such as hūṃ will also arise.
If in the disciple and the others
A subtle descent arises at the heart,
Then with the mudrā of Vajrahūṃkāra
The disciple should be brought in.
Having brought him in, one should recite
The First Being's own heart mantra.

hūṃ hūṃ vajra praharaṇi phaṭ hūṃ vajra śūlayāgriṇi triti mata sa hūṃ vajrakoṣe cchindda haṃ oṃ vajra amala pāśe maṭa

The vajra summoner, the vajra spear, the vajra sword and the vajra noose: these are the protection of Vajrakrodha.

Then one should rouse the mudrās of these, of unfailing vajra and unobstructed, thoroughly accomplished, powerful and overpowering adversaries, able to seal beings completely.

oṃ vajra guhya siddha parama yogīśvari hi hūṃ vajra caṇḍa ho khaṭvāṃga mahāvajra kapāla yam kute rulu rulu ṇṭi hūṃ vajra nārāyaṇi ji hūṃ vajra vaṭake ji

These are the ḍākinīs of Vajrakrodha. Having recited them once, one should set them out or draw them. If one invites the Blessed One to be seated with the heart mantra of suchness, the accomplishment that is unfailing in all accomplishments will be attained.

Then one should pronounce the commitment:

"For the happiness of many beings
And for the accomplishment of the whole teaching,
From taming the wicked, merit
Arises." So the Vajra Holder declared.

Then one should join the finger together with the mudrā of the Blessed One Vajrahūṃkāra. Then the Blessed One blessed it to be the thorough taming of all the wicked, all the māras, and all the three worlds.

Then, having performed the extensive empowerment and the rest in the same way, one should train in the four activities of Vajrakrodha: sealing beings with the mudrās, undoing the mudrā, killing, and restoring to life. If the one to whom the mudrā is applied dies, it should be understood that he is wicked. Then one should revive him with the reviving mudrā; and if one applies thorough protection, because he is then thoroughly protected, the wicked will not harm him again.

From then on, the good disciple
Cannot be overcome or slain
By any of the vajra hosts,
And will attain accomplishment in this very life.
Then I shall explain the practice here:
With the body as is fitting, amid the Saṅgha,
Brandishing the vajra, one should first
Perform a hundred thousand recitations.
When the hundred thousand is complete, the great being,
Supreme, equal to Vajrasattva,
The supreme king Vajrakrodha,
Will be free of aging and death.
He tames the wicked,
Makes abundant offerings to all the buddhas,
Shines splendidly in the three worlds,
And becomes fearless of anything at all.

Then the practice of the heart mantra of suchness:

Whether standing or else sitting,
Walking or lying down,
If, however one is, one recites it a hundred thousand times,
One will attain accomplishment in this very life.
Through this, as lord of all desires,
By mere wish one goes, comes and soars;
One attains lordship over all accomplishments
And becomes free of aging and death.
Worshiped by beings
And attended by one's own kin,
One tames all the wicked
And moves about fearless of anything.

So spoke the Blessed One, the great king of the wrathful ones.

Then the practice of the supreme mudrā:

At the proper time, bind the mudrā.
Having surely burned oneself,
For as long as one does so, all the accomplishments
Of the Great Wrathful One will be attained.
Then the practice of the commitment:
Saying, "I shall slay them all,"
And binding the commitment mudrā
Of Vajrapāṇi,
If one recites, one will attain the Vajra Holder.
Then the practice of the activities:
The practice of the supreme activities
Is performed in four ways;
If one eats for the happiness of all,
One will attain Vajrakarma itself.

This is the tantra of the extensive rite of the great practice.

Then the practice of accomplishments:

The supreme tantras of the activities
Of the king, the supreme Vajra Wrathful One,
I shall explain in their proper sequence.

Namely: for burning all, expelling, destroying, paralyzing, putting to sleep, sealing, stupefying, destroying, binding, tormenting, tearing apart, beating, setting ablaze, felling, cooking, cutting, piercing, pulverizing, scattering, utterly crushing, causing one to be seized by fever, poison and grahas, maddening, and killing, it is done by Vajrahūṃkāra.

For summoning all, setting them firmly in place, moving them, making them dance, making them sing, making them faint, making them play, making them laugh "ha ha," whirling, drawing in, making them shine, meditating, making them sink, expelling, hurling, lifting up, sending, making them roar, making them weep, making them beat themselves, binding, making them tear out their hair, making them hostile, stripping off their clothes, and making wrath blaze up, it is done by the mudrā of the seat of the wrathful one.

For summoning all, causing entry, causing descent, accomplishing a prasena divination, making one speak the truth, displaying illusions, giving answers, reversing the wheel of the seasons, displaying trees ripening flowers and fruit out of season, properly displaying all grains, freeing from fever, compounded poison, grahas, poison and ḍākinīs, freeing from all battles, crushing the armies of others, binding the directions, the boundaries and the maṇḍala, thorough protection, complete safeguarding, liberating, raising up, and bringing under control, it is done by the Great Wrathful One of Manifold Accomplishment.

For making all the gods and others fall, exorcising nāgas, making yakṣas follow, terrifying rākṣasas, subduing all grahas such as ḍākinīs, crushing all the armies of Māra, taming the mātṛkās, scattering the hosts of the wicked, annihilating the wicked, reconciling, properly generating fearlessness, bringing Maṇibhadra under control, seizing all wealth from the Giver of Wealth (Kubera), pressing down Vaiśravaṇa and drawing away his fortune, sporting with all the young daughters of the gods, pressing down Śakra and seizing Śacī, annihilating Skanda, making Brahmā fall, taming Viṣṇu, conquering Maheśvara, pressing down the Lord of Desire and seizing Rati, accomplishing the vajra, accomplishing great awakening, pleasing all the tathāgatas, summoning, causing entry, binding by the commitment, and accomplishing control, it is done by the accomplishment of the great Vajrakrodha, the supreme king. This too he declared.

By reciting once, the supreme is accomplished:
First, the excellent root heart mantra once;
After that, with a wrathful frown,
All that is to be accomplished is summoned.

From the great king of rituals, Śrī Paramādya, this is the extensive rite of the ritual blazing intensely like vajra, fire and sun.

Then, of all the samādhis,
From the nature of reality, supremely pure,
The great Avalokiteśvara
Is the great chief of Vajrasattva.
In order to tame all the wicked,
When precious self-awareness is generated,
The great wrathful one of all the buddhas,
Through no means at all, is itself the means.
The realms of impure beings,
Amassed through desire and the rest:
How to act fully among them
The supreme person knows.
Because they are supremely untamable,
Even the buddhas hold no sway over them.
The supreme of the world in all worlds
Himself enters equipoise in the excellent.

Here, this is the king of samādhis of all the tathāgatas, the samādhi called the Vajra Blessing of the Wisdom of the Nature of Reality in Which All Phenomena Are Pure by Nature. By beholding it, all the tathāgatas came to realize the very lordship over all things in all the three realms.

Proudly holding a lotus in his left hand
And opening its petals,
Through the samādhi of all that he is able,
He looked upon it by its very nature:
"Just as this red lotus
Is unstained by the faults of its dye,
So the three realms, with the manifold
Faults of their abodes, are likewise pure."
Through looking upon this and the rest,
The victors became lords.
Through it Vajrasattva
Became Avalokiteśvara.
Then, when one meditates on this heart of meditation,
Meditating on it purely,
The great Vajradhara
Becomes the holder of the great lotus.
Equal in luster to the great king,
The lord of mountains, of refined gold,
Proudly holding the supreme lotus in the left,
He gazes upon it with the supreme right.
Then on the petals of the lotus,
Holding the supreme great lotus,
The wheels from which buddhas and the rest arise,
The supreme among tamers,
Who tame the manifold beings,
Should be well drawn, seated there,
In the manner of the great maṇḍala.
When, there upon that virtuous lotus,
He was taming them by the method
Of thoroughly purifying all beings,
So that he would teach the supreme lotus family,
The victors made their request.
Then the one who tames all,
The holder of the utterly white Dharma,
Vajradharma, himself the king,
Lokeśvara, declared:
"Outside, in the great maṇḍala,
Draw a lotus of eight petals.
In its center, with smiling eyes,
Lokeśvara should be placed,
Gazing at a lotus like refined gold,
Held proudly in the left hand,
Adorned with every victory banner,
With a supreme buddha form seated on his crown.
On all of its sides
Draw the eight buddha forms,
Gazing upon the samādhi of the great lotus,
Their own samādhi.
Beside the form of Lokeśvara
Are the deities of the family, the buddha and the rest:
Śiva, Nārāyaṇa, Brahmā,
The lotus-bearing form of Desire,
And Hayagrīva, shining at the crown, his neck raised.
Having bound the hook,
Invite them with the words of the Dharma."
Then the heart mantra of the lotus:

sarvatathāgata padma jaḥ hūṃ vajrapadma he padma padma vaṃ ho padmani rāja

Then, in the intermediate directions:

hūṃ padme caṇḍeśvara hūṃ he mahāviśā padma cūrṇe siddha hūṃ maṭa hrīḥ padma padmo hūṃ aho padma aśva krama krama hūṃ jaḥ

In the manner of the great maṇḍala,
Having performed the extensive rite,
With a great lotus flower
One should pay homage with undistracted mind.
Having entered the maṇḍala, one should recite
This dhāraṇī arisen from samādhi:

oṃ viśodhaya manusani śuddha mahāsatva dhātu samaya satva dāgadī pañcayi citta me sarva dha me ṣṭye vajriṇi citta me sarva dha me śya jini

When he has heard the seal of the dhāraṇī,
Delighted, the Lotus Holder is accomplished.
Then bind the fingers into a hook,
And invite him well with the words of the Dharma:

oṃ sarvatathāgata avalokiśvara padmahasta yahi śīghraṃ mame vajramudrā agriśya save samayer vadha viśi komi jaḥ hūṃ vaṃ hoḥ śodhaya sarva sādhaya va mame mahāvajra dtar stāṃ

Then the supreme Protector of the World,
The Upholder of the World, comes in person,
And surely performs the rite of samādhi
Through which the All-Supreme of all worlds will be accomplished.
Then, likewise, in the extensive rite
Of bringing in the disciples,
They should be trained in the wisdoms
That grant all accomplishments.
The great practice of this heart of samādhi,
Which arose from the very beginning,
The lordship over all worlds,
I shall explain in its proper sequence.
Whether one has entered among women,
Or gone among the wicked,
Or stays wherever one may,
Take up the great lotus.
In keeping with time and strength,
One should practice each day as much as one can.
Through this rite, in this very life,
The supreme will swiftly be accomplished.
Swift in the bliss of every desire,
By mere wish one goes, comes and soars;
A victorious lord of all worlds,
One becomes free of aging and death.
Buddhahood, bodhisattvahood
And the state of Vajrapāṇi,
The supreme, infinite in all accomplishments,
Will be attained in this very life.
Then the practice of the mudrā:
Binding the mudrā and abiding in samādhi,
One attains the great accomplishment itself.
The commitment is through the supreme commitment mudrā;
The activity, through the supreme activity mudrā.

This is the tantra of the rite of the great practice.

Then the activities of samādhi
That draw forth accomplishments not yet attained:
First, the accomplishment of samādhi
I shall explain in its proper sequence.

Namely: for all obscurations, misdeeds, bad dreams, ill omens, epidemics, harm, contagious disease, afflictions, untimely death, fear, terror, disputes, fights, child-seizing grahas, brigands, afflictions, weariness, suffering, unhappiness, poverty, arrogance, pain at a touch, ill fortune, sickness and loss of wealth, it is done by the Peaceful Great Lotus.

For curing wind, bile, blood, phlegm, their combination, indigestion, fever, compounded poison, poison, leprosy, small pustules, itch, sores of the feet, tumors, dropsy, fistula, leukoderma, wasting disease, depletion of blood, depletion of semen, all diseases, harms, harmful spirits, grahas and witches, it is done by the Great Lotus who holds the mudrā of medicine.

For making all into vajra, and for Dharma, armor, the uṣṇīṣa, the crest, the boundaries, the directions, the maṇḍala, the ground, the sky, the vajra seat, the great house, the vajra fence, blazing fire, the enclosure of ash, the vajra, the Unfailing Mountain and the rest, encircling with thread, staking with the kīla, food, drink, clothing, ornaments, mudrā and protection, it is done by the All-Protecting Great Lotus.

For accomplishing all life, strength, complexion, splendor, radiance, youth, diligence, enthusiasm, health, glory, good fortune, wealth, grain, lordship, prosperity, accomplishment, intelligence, gain, auspiciousness, name, renown, fame, samādhi and supreme wisdom, it is done by the Supremely Accomplished Great Lotus.

From the great king of rituals, Śrī Paramādya, this is the extensive rite of the vajra of the wisdom in which all phenomena are unobscured.

Then the king, the Vajra Holder,
Great space, wholly unchanging,
Who empowers all, the aim of all,
Lord of all, holder of every jewel,
Blessed through samādhi,
Fully awakened the great Rudra.
Through his gazing upon phenomena,
The lord of the aims of all arose from the very beginning,
And, with sun and moon, planets and stars,
Became space itself.
Then the samādhi of the jewel
Is surely declared to be the Vajra Bearer.
Then space-vajra itself,
Equal in luster to blue-green beryl,
The virtuous one holding the vajra jewel,
Truly became Ākāśagarbha.
Then this Blessed One, the first,
Is seated at the place of the ūrṇā of all.
Through his gazing upon samādhi,
Lokeśvara, the lord himself,
All the buddhas together with their heirs,
And likewise the supreme Vajra Holder,
Through that Vajraratna,
Were empowered by all the all-knowing ones;
And so that every intention might be fulfilled,
The All-Supreme, having empowered all,
Spoke this utterance of auspiciousness:

oṃ sarvavajrasatva atipati stvaṃ abhiṣiñcāmi bhur bhuva svaḥ

Then, when the Excellent One had spoken this
And empowered with well-spoken words of auspiciousness,
From that great Vajraratna
He arose as the lord of the Dharma.
Having arisen, he empowered
All beings, each as was fitting.
Having been empowered by all the vajras,
He again sat in the manner of the maṇḍala.
Blessed by all the sugatas,
The unsurpassed Vajraratna,
Supreme among the very great lords,
Spoke the ritual for bestowing accomplishments:
"Outside the great maṇḍala,
Draw a wheel of space, the color of space,
Adorned with the sun and moon, the stars
And the planets.
In its center, Ākāśagarbha,
The self in which all delight, should be placed,
Colored like pure beryl,
Swirling with manifold blazing rays,
His left hand raising a jewel at his heart,
His right bestowing great wealth.
On its sides are the supreme beings,
The four, excellent, with their dhāraṇī goddesses:
Empowerment, Treasure,
The Great Unsurpassed Jewel,
And Perfect Glory of Prosperity,
Worshiped with their own emblems."

Here, these are their heart mantras: vajrasatva jaḥ vajraratna hūṃ vajradharma vaṃ vajrakarma hoḥ.

Then the heart mantras of the dhāraṇī goddesses are taught: oṃ prativajriṇi phaṭ oṃ vajrabrīti hi hi oṃ cintāmaṇi hoḥ oṃ vajra mahāmahe haṃ.

In the manner of the great maṇḍala,
Perform the extensive rite as prescribed.
With the jewel fist make a clear request,
And bow your head to the Sky-Born.
Then, seated, one should utter
The excellent commitment of the one skilled in space:
"You, who arose from space,
Are the fifth of all."

oṃ i siddha bhur bhuga siddha siddha svaḥ siddha mahāvajraratna va va tra ṭvā trāṃ

By merely reciting this,
All the accomplishments of Ākāśagarbha
And every perfection
Will surely be attained; of this there is no doubt.

So spoke the Blessed One, the unsurpassed Vajraratna.

Then one should summon with the hook of the one with the jewel fist:

oṃ bhūrāyahījā bhuva vra viśa hūṃ sva gṛhṇa vaṃ va pa ṭa ṭa siddha ho trāṃ

Then the Blessed One, coming swiftly,
Will cause all accomplishments
And prosperity to stir abundantly,
And one's aims will be fully accomplished.
Then the disciple, by the supreme mudrā
Together with a flower, is brought in.
Having brought him in, have him recite once
The supreme heart of suchness itself, the truth.
Then, examining the supreme of prosperity,
With great offerings of perfect glory,
He attains, like a sugata,
The accomplishments of the supreme of the world.
Then, for this, the practice is explained:
Through the supreme king Vajraratna,
One should practice by countless repetitions.
Through this, in this very supreme birth,
One will come and go as one desires;
A victorious lord of all jewels,
One becomes free of aging and death.

So it is, likewise, with all the excellent accomplishments. The practice of the mudrā is through joy itself. The practice of the commitment mudrā is through the commitment fist. Likewise, the practice of the activity is the unsurpassed accomplishment. This is the tantra of the very practice of the great mudrā.

Then, for this, the vast and excellent cause
Of the activities,
And the great accomplishments in which all is accomplished,
I shall explain in their proper sequence.

Namely: for summoning all prosperity, causing it to enter, binding it, bringing it under control, seizing it, amassing it, taking it, leading it, drawing it in, bestowing it, destroying, hurling, lifting up, bringing down, striking, purifying, finding, buying, selling, distributing everywhere, setting in place, making firm, making it vajra, accomplishing all prosperity, and accomplishing the supreme of all prosperity, it is done by the empowerment of Vajraratna.

For summoning all glory, causing it to enter, binding it, bringing it under control, seizing it, amassing it, taking it, leading it, drawing it in, bestowing it, destroying, hurling, lifting up, bringing down, striking, purifying, finding, buying, selling, distributing everywhere, setting in place, making firm, making it vajra, accomplishing all prosperity, and accomplishing the supreme of all prosperity, it is done by the Glory of Vajraratna.

For summoning all perfections, causing them to enter, binding them, bringing them under control, seizing them, amassing them, taking them, leading them, drawing them in, bestowing them, destroying, hurling, lifting up, bringing down, striking, purifying, finding, buying, selling, distributing everywhere, setting in place, making firm, making them vajra, accomplishing all prosperity, and accomplishing the supreme of all prosperity, it is done by the Perfection of Vajraratna.

For summoning all offerings, causing them to enter, binding them, bringing them under control, seizing them, amassing them, taking them, leading them, drawing them in, bestowing them, destroying, hurling, lifting up, bringing down, striking, purifying, finding, buying, selling, distributing everywhere, setting in place, making firm, making them vajra, accomplishing all prosperity, and accomplishing the supreme of all prosperity, it is done by accomplishing the offerings of Vajraratna. This too he declared.

For the happiness of all beings,
Vajrasattva, rich in great wealth,
Should accomplish his own supreme victor once,
And apply the syllable traṃ.

From the great king of rituals, Śrī Paramādya, this is the extensive rite of the ritual of Vajraratna, which completely fulfills every intention.

Then the Victor, the Vajra Holder,
The three worlds, the three realms,
Who among all the lords of the three worlds
Tames the supreme ones of the three worlds,
Meditated according to the rite,
And for the benefit of beings abides by the rite.
Desire, aversion and delusion
Always remain.
If they are relied upon improperly,
They become poison itself;
If they are relied upon as nectar,
They become nectar itself.
Desire brings forth the supreme Brahmā;
Aversion, through the samādhi of Rudra;
And delusion, having made Viṣṇu firm,
Blessed the three worlds.
Because desire, aversion and delusion
Always remain, the three existences remain.
Relied on with the Dharma, they are the three supreme;
Relied on without the Dharma, the three lower realms.
Desire is called the Honey Maker;
Aversion is called the Victory Maker;
Delusion is called the Accomplisher of Aims:
These are their wholly secret names.
"I alone am the world,
Proclaimed as the three worlds.
I alone am desire:
Brahmā, Rudra and the Eternal One."
Then the supreme of these three,
In order to purify them, the Holder of the purifying vajra:
These three forms themselves
Arose from the self alone.
For these worlds
Always say this:
"Those tamed by Brahmā and the others
Are not vessels for the true awakening of a buddha."
To purify them, the Blessed One,
The great Vajradhara himself,
Took on these three forms,
And afterward his buddhahood does not change.
Then Brahmā, the gods and the other gods,
And Maheśvara, supreme in the three worlds,
Offered that maṇḍala, the secret heart,
For their own accomplishment.

oṃ bhūr bhuva svaḥ

Four-cornered, with four gates,
Well guarded by four gatekeepers,
Well encircled by a rim,
The outer maṇḍala should be drawn.
Within it,
In the bow-shaped maṇḍala,
The Honey Maker and the Victory Maker,
And the Accomplisher of all that is to be accomplished,
In their proper sequence,
Should be well drawn or placed.
Kneeling on both knees, make a clear request there;
Marked with the trident of the commitment,
Having entered and bound the commitment,
One should properly summon.

oṃ bhūr bhuva svaḥ svasti nāma svāhā

By reciting it twenty-one times,
All, surrounded by all,
Come for the accomplishment of all;
Give tormas to them, as many as they are.
The master, having fully entered,
Should bring in all the disciples.
Having told them the secret of suchness,
He should give the supreme commitment:
"If you break the trident,
If you speak the heart mantra to those who are not vessels,
Or if you do not practice here
The teachings just as they were spoken!
Sattva, rajas and tamas,
Brahmā, Rudra and the Eternal One:
These three brothers
Must never be despised."
Whoever trains in meditating on these,
Entering again and again
And worshiping the maṇḍala,
Will attain all accomplishments.
Having trained in one's own mudrā,
With offerings of dance to it
And with the five excellent sense pleasures,
One should always worship the maṇḍala.

This is the tantra of bringing forth the three worlds.

Then the Teacher, the Vajra Holder,
Who guides all beings,
Having brought forth the three worlds to accomplish their aims,
Then brought forth the sisters.
Utter desire, aversion and delusion
Are the cause that makes them firm.
The women, joy and jealousy,
Grant renowned accomplishments.
Those who are proclaimed throughout the world,
The goddesses worshiped by all the hosts,
Are called the Peaceful, the Terrifying, likewise Gold,
The Accomplished and the Peaceful.
For the benefit of beings outside,
And so that the meaning to be realized may be understood,
Their heart mantras,
Their maṇḍala and likewise their commitment are explained.

hrī hūṃ śrī bhyo

The excellent maṇḍala has four gates;
Make the maṇḍala with three threads.
The boundary lines are great pillars,
The four in sequence, as stated.
In its center, the maṇḍalas,
Four of them, are made likewise.
Their own four heart deities
Are placed facing their own gates:
Subhagā, holding a sword in hand;
The sister with a shield, Śivā;
Vārāhī, with treasure in hand;
And Siddhā, full of pride.
Then, into the first maṇḍala,
Having likewise entered completely,
With the sisters' own seals,
In their proper sequence,
Having entered, likewise according to the rite,
One should worship with incense and the rest.
By fully practicing the secret of suchness,
One should summon them from all sides.

oṃ sumabhaga ratiṣṭhamaya hi bhūr rakṣasa mātiṣṣāmaya hi bhūr dhuva barāhā amiryātamaya svāhā siddhike siddhi samaya hi īmāṃ ke mudrābhiḥ hrīḥ hūṃ śrī bhyo

Then, with the whole body,
Likewise sit well,
And then, showing the bound mudrā,
One should utter this supreme secret:
"If you do not guard the secret itself,
Whoever has entered for the benefit of beings
And whoever practices the maṇḍala,
You will have slain them.
For one endowed with the Dharma it is nectar itself;
For one without the Dharma it is worse than poison."
However much one wishes, and according to the time,
Relying on the sense objects
And with the offerings of mudrā and dance,
Always worship as one pleases.

This is the tantra of the four sisters.

Then the Teacher, the Vajra Holder,
Supreme among all the lords of the earth,
The great attainment and the supreme attainment
Of attachment, aversion, and delusion,
In order to destroy the poisons of attachment and the rest,
And to turn them wholly into nectar,
The supreme maṇḍala of the nāgas,
Arising from aversion, comes forth.
Its heart mantra is this: phuḥ

Four-cornered, with four gates,
All its lines should be drawn correctly.
The gateway arches are the supreme hoods of nāgas;
The supreme points of the corners are knotted tails.
In its center, the nāga king
Ananta, together with his hoods,
And the eight families of the nāgas
The wise one should draw all around.
Then, entering in wrath,
By the force of the one who holds the nāgas,
Having entered into that very maṇḍala,
One should summon them with the serpent's hood.

oṃ hrīḥ anīlajavavegapṛthibhinadaṛṣeśāyahi phuḥ sarva nāga kulānyānaya phuḥ samaya praveśaya phuḥ sarvāna sādhaya phuḥ tatasa sarva nāga putraka phuḥ

When this is done, they will be well settled and delighted. Then the master should regard himself as free of poison. From then on, even if he eats any poison whatsoever, it turns into nectar. He sports with Vāsuki. He becomes able to kindle all poisons and to destroy them.

Then one should give the heart of the oath:

"Attachment, aversion, and delusion,
These three are poison in the world.
If you transgress the truth,
They will always slay you.
You must not scorn the lightning,
You must not turn back the hail,
And if you delight in accomplishment,
By no means should you kill a snake."

This is the maṇḍala of the nāgas.

Then the Teacher, the Vajra Holder,
Supreme dispeller of all suffering,
For the happiness of all sentient beings,
Brought forth the maṇḍala of the grahas.
Attachment, aversion, and delusion,
Through misdeeds, become poison itself;
They become grahas, fevers, and evil,
And even become the hells.
Then the heart of the vicious is this: haṃ

Four-cornered, with four gates,
The maṇḍala of the grahas should be drawn all around.
In its center, first, the grahas and the rest
Should be expelled through the elaborate rite.
Then, having entered as was explained,
Laughing, and swiftly, into the maṇḍala,
The Vajra Holder, having entered,
Wrathfully tramples with the left foot.
Then grahas, fevers, and evil,
All the hells, and even those
Who always commit great evil,
Are utterly destroyed by the vajra.

Thus spoke the Blessed One, Vajrasattva. This is the tantra of deliverance from all suffering.

Then the Teacher, the Vajra Holder,
The supreme lord of the All-Supreme,
In order to accomplish the aims of all sentient beings,
Taught all the maṇḍalas.
Their heart mantra is this: oṃ sāḥ hūṃ hrīḥ trāṃ

Then, merely by his uttering this,
The supreme lord of the All-Supreme,
Through all forms, became
The supreme lord of the All-Supreme.
When beings were not tamed
By the five great secrets,
Then he acts on a great and varied scale:
He is the supreme tamer of beings.

Then, for this reason, the great
And supreme maṇḍala shall be properly explained.
Having drawn the great maṇḍala
Of the supreme lord of the All-Supreme,
The manifold, the completely victorious,
In its center, according to the rite,
One should set Vajrasattva,
With the maṇḍala of the host of vajra goddesses.
On all of its sides
Are the bodies of the Lord of the All-Supreme.
Their emblems, together with supreme vajras,
Should be placed, encircled by their own families.
Draw King Vajra Nārāyaṇa
In his form, holding a wheel;
The All-Pervading Lord, the Vajra Bearer, with a vajra;
The Fierce Lord (Caṇḍeśvara), holding a vajra spear;
Vajra Padmodbhava, holding a lotus;
The Vajra Holder, bearing the form of Brahmā;
Ākāśagarbha, in the form of the god of desire,
With a vajra, his weapon of flowers.
In the corners of the maṇḍala
One should draw the goddesses of the families:
Vajraśrī and Gaurī,
Vajratārā and Sky Vajra.

Then the heart mantras of these lords of the maṇḍala shall be explained:

oṃ vajra nārāyaṇa cchinda pratihana vajra cakra tena ho bhagavāna vajra tathāgata jaḥ hūṃ vaṃ hoḥ vajradhāra taṃ vajra tathāgata hūṃ vajra krodha mahāyogini gaṇeśvara mahāvajra kulogra pane ho bhagavāna vajra caṇḍeśvarāya ha ha ha ha hrīḥ vajra padmodabhava ho bhagavāna vajra lokeśvare mahā prame siddhya oṃ bhūra bhuva svaḥ svasti namaḥ svāhā he vajra kusumāyudha sarva kāma me sādhaya ho bhagavāna vajra ākāśagarbha va va ṭa ṭa traṃ vajra ākāśagarbha aśeṣaṃ mudrā namo yena sādhena bhagavate prajñāpāramitāyudha cchinda vajraṇi cakreṇa tenastyen pāpakana oṃ dhīḥ śrī śruti smṛti vijaye svāhā vajraparmi oṃ vajra gauri mahāvidya trailokya vijayeśvari vajra krodha agani samaye vajradhari namostute tadyathā oṃ hili mili leli tāli vajra kamukaye svāhā vajra samaya namo yena sādhyena bhagavati buddha bodhirbuddhadhara vajradharma prayogena tenastyena sidhamāṃ oṃ tāre tuttare ture svāhā vajra dhamota oṃ karma vajriṇi sidbhya sidbhya viśuddha sādhya vaha gaganodbhave yogena siddhamāṃ paramakare tadyathā gagana viśuddhe sarva mava priya paripūrni svāhā

In the places of the outer maṇḍala
The Vajra Holders dwell in their own directions.
The lords of the gods, the mātṛkās,
And the world guardians should be set out.
The lords of the hosts, beating
The ten great instruments of the vajra,
Should be drawn at the portals of the outer maṇḍala,
And at its gates and corners as well.
At all of the gates, at the entrances,
Their own mudrās should be placed.
Into the fifth supreme maṇḍala
One should enter through the first union.

Then, being seated in the center,
First, the great Aṅkuśī herself:
Reciting this heart mantra,
One should utter this summons:

oṃ mahāsukha vajrasatva siddhi hūṃ sarva tathāgatan samaya ākarṣaya jaḥ vajra kula dipaṃ damayetva praveśaya hūṃ sarva lokeśvaraṃ praveśaya samaya vanadhana vaṃ samaya sarva ratna dīpaṃ mahāmaṇi rājānāṃ vaśī kuru hoḥ hūṃ āḥ hūṃ hrīḥ trāṃ

Then, by uttering this excellent
Heart mantra of the great secret,
The king, the great Vajradhara,
Together with all the circles,
Is summoned and caused to enter,
Bound by truth and brought under control.
All the supreme kings of maṇḍalas
Will be accomplished with every accomplishment.
Then, in the proper sequence,
One should accomplish all the maṇḍalas,
And all of those accomplishments too
Should be accomplished, every one.
All bliss abides unchanging:
It is called Great Bliss.
This vajra, its accomplishment and the rest,
Will be accomplished in this very life.

Thus has been taught the rite whose aim is to purify manifold aims, which tames immeasurable beings; endless and manifold, unequaled and preeminent, holding all the families of the tathāgatas and the rest, producing supreme bliss, blessed by all the victors, and producing the supreme bliss of existence.

From the Śrī Paramādya, King of Ritual Manuals: the elaborate maṇḍala rite of the supreme lord of the All-Supreme, from the root tantra of the later tantra of all the ritual manuals, is complete.

Then the secret rite of the painted cloth is taught:

The great secret of all the buddhas,
The accomplishment of the supreme samaya,
The celestial palace of great bliss,
Its body formed of every accomplishment,
Adorned with the kings of jewels and the rest
Of the great and supreme tathāgatas,
With songs and music suffused
With the passion of great bliss,
Is utterly worshiped with all the offerings
Of unsurpassed great joy.
In that supreme dwelling of his own,
The abode of great joy and glory,
The supreme king of utter joy,
The supreme unchanging one, is properly seated.
Vajrasattva, my very self,
Utterly joyful, supreme and unchanging;
Samantabhadra, the retinue,
The bodhisattvas and the tathāgatas;
Supreme among all great accomplishments,
Vajra Pride, lord of lords,
The lord of accomplishment, accomplished in accord
With the great passion of the All-Supreme;
Songs and music without beginning or end:
The supreme was proclaimed by the Blessed One.
hūṃ

Then, merely by his uttering this,
From the holder of all vajras,
The great reality of the five secrets,
The goddesses of the mudrās came forth:
Constant passion and great accomplishment,
The victorious, the sovereign, the surpassing goddess,
These are the four supreme mudrās,
Bodies blessed by the sattva.

Then their heart is this:

"The discernment of the All-Supreme,
The supreme bliss of every accomplishment,
The sight of the victory of the All-Supreme,
Or the great worship of the lord of all;
The accomplishments and the perfections,
The joys and the lords,
The vajra goddesses without beginning or end,
Supreme of the body of Vajrasattva."

So it is said.

Then the supremely secret names are these: Kāmavajrī, of the samaya of the joy of the Great Joy, the glorious Vajra Amogha; Vajra Kilikili, of the samaya of passion for the Great Joy, the glorious Amogha; Vijayavajrī, of the samaya of passion for the Great Joy, the glorious Vajra Amogha; and Vajrakāmeśvarī, of the samaya of passion for the Great Joy, the glorious Vajra Amogha.

Whatever wise one, with faith,
Recites these secret names
Becomes the lord, the master,
Of every accomplishment of all the mudrās.

Thus spoke the Blessed One Vajrasattva, the Supremely Secret.

Then its accomplishment shall be explained,
An accomplishment most carefully examined:
Even those who delight in great evil,
And whatever practices have not succeeded,
Just as Vajrasattva has taught,
These too will swiftly succeed.
Each day, or each month,
Or likewise each year,
One should accomplish whatever one wishes for.
With whatever can be obtained, and as is comfortable,
One should draw the Blessed One on cloth.
The lord of all, the lord of the samaya:
Whoever draws him
Attains supreme, everlasting liberation.

Smiling, with the light of the moon,
Blazing with the light of variegated lotuses,
With the consecration of all the buddhas and the rest,
Endowed with various ornaments and garments,
Seated in the sattva cross-legged posture,
Holding the supreme vajra of pride,
His left hand proudly bearing the supreme bell:
Thus should Vajrasattva be visualized.
Those who are seated at his sides,
Resting within the maṇḍala of his light,
With bodies of utter joy and bliss,
The great secret ones, should be set out.
The first is white and red like a lotus;
Another is white like the palāśa;
Another is blue like an utpala petal;
The Vajra Proud One is yellow and holds her emblem.
The first and the second
Rest on the right side;
The third and the fourth
Are seated on the left side.
Vajra Kilikili
Delights her own deity in the center, emanating joy.
At her side, the one intent on fitting
The arrow of the great vajra,
Embracing with a proud hand,
Together with a raised makara banner.
At her side, smiling,
Gazing at the lord with offerings,
Bearing the mark of the jewel consecration of the buddhas,
Looking with the mudrā of all eyes;
With her mind fixed upon the lord in herself,
She is seated, glancing sideways.

By seeing this painting
One will attain everlasting merit.
Through offering and contemplation
One attains the liberation of supreme joy.

Thus spoke the Blessed One Vajrasattva.

Thus, one who intensely desires supreme joy
Accomplishes the pure and sublime accomplishment of supreme joy.
This great painting of the Victor,
Clear and completely victorious,
With great worship of the all-seeing ones,
One revels here in glory.
Having contemplated that circle,
The supremely secret maṇḍala,
In a secluded house,
Or else in a pleasure grove or the like,
Adorned with the painting of the great and supreme tathāgata,
The king, and the rest,
And, by offering a delightful canopy
Hung with bells, made supreme,
With the offerings of vajra song and the rest,
With displays of song and music,
With flowers, incense and the like well arranged,
And with lamps set out, one should worship correctly.
In a maṇḍala in a three-storied house
One should accomplish the supreme secret.
Or else one should meditate on oneself alone,
The supreme one, as one's own chosen deity.
Then, even without entering the circle,
Having always entered it by oneself,
The master who holds all vajras
Will succeed in every accomplishment.
Having bathed oneself and anointed oneself with fragrance,
Endowed with as many ornaments and garments as one has,
Properly clad in varied clothing,
With a garland, and with fragrant mouth,
Through the yoga of Vajrasattva,
With flowers, incense and vessels of scent,
And with these vajra songs,
One should dance and enter the city.

For this, here is the supremely accomplished worship by the supremely secret song for entering the glorious three samayas of the supreme secret:

"Into the city of liberation, delightful with the bliss of every accomplishment,
The Blessed One, Great Bliss, enters.
Through the sublime accomplishment of supreme bliss,
jaḥ hūṃ vaṃ hoḥrab, grant perfect accomplishment!"

Having fully entered, with the left,
With vajra eyes one bows to the Protector:
The left proud, smiling slightly,
Praising, with beautiful eyes half closed.

For this, here is the supremely accomplished worship by the supremely secret vajra song for scattering the flowers of homage of the glorious three samayas of the sublime supreme secret:

"With a mind of supreme bliss, endowed with play,
With a graceful hand, I bow to the Blessed One.
jaḥ hūṃ vaṃ hoḥ hi hi hi hi hi
Protector, please accept this handful of flowers!"

Then, having scattered the flowers,
With the empowerment of Vajrasattva,
Endowed with the mudrā of vajra and bell,
One should bestow the clear meaning.

For this, here is the supremely accomplished worship by the supremely secret vajra song of the empowerment of Vajrasattva of the glorious three samayas of the sublime supreme secret:

"oṃ Endowed with all the buddhas, adorned with supreme jewels,
Together with the crest of the gods, I bow to you.
Through the jewel empowerment of the lord Vajradhara,
Vajrasattva, samaya stvaṃ haṃ."

With that very mantra one should take up the vajra
And ring the bell of the Dharma.
With the samaya, having blessed the great mudrā,
One should recite the heart mantra.
By obtaining and holding the vajra and the rest,
One is like a second Vajrasattva,
The king who holds all vajras,
Blessed by the Vajra Bearer.

Thus the Blessed One Vajrasattva taught the supremely accomplished rite of the solitary practice of blessing oneself through the glorious three samayas of the sublime supreme secret.

Then its accomplishment here shall be explained,
An accomplishment that is utterly constant:
Whoever is a sublime practitioner,
And even those who commit great evil,
Just as Vajrasattva has taught,
Even the unaccomplished will be accomplished.
Whether cast or hammered,
Or assembled, or painted,
Having invited the First Being to be seated,
Before him, according to the rite,
Always holding vajra and bell,
One should accomplish the supreme accomplishment:
Summoning all, great worship,
Blessing oneself and the rest are the practices.

For this, the tantra of summoning all the accomplishments of the glorious three samayas of the sublime supreme secret is this:

Out of the yoga of one's own chosen deity,
Through the yoga of summoning all,
With supreme limbs, together with play,
One should dance and should summon.

Then, the worship by the supremely secret samaya song of the supreme accomplishment of all the tathāgatas is this:

"jaḥ Supreme, primordial Vajradhara, Vajrasattva, fully realized in bliss, utterly joyful, you, my lord, discern the buddhas individually.
hūṃ Supreme Vajrakarma, supremely unchanging, inexhaustible, fully accomplishing existence, grant me today bliss, happiness of mind, and the samaya!
vaṃ Supreme, holding my body, raising the victory of all yogas, utterly supreme and pure, O king of lotuses who brings about bliss!
hoḥ Utterly supreme: may I become the sublime lord of all the sublime supreme accomplishments!
You, Ākāśagarbha, who ever increase me,
Bring them down through the practice of hulu hulu!
You who rightly summon through the great samaya,
The samaya of all the tathāgatas,
Supremely joyful, supreme lord!"

For this, what is the rite of the great worship of all the accomplishments of the glorious three samayas of the sublime supreme secret?

In accordance with the yoga of one's own deity,
Worship with all offerings the bodies of the All-Supreme;
Having worshiped, the mantra practitioner
Then worships again with displays of songs and music.

Then, the worship by the supremely secret samaya vajra song of the supreme rite of the great worship of the supreme accomplishment by all the tathāgatas is this:

"Mind of sublime bliss, passionate toward all,
Vajrasattva, you are utter joy.
May clear vision of Great Bliss become firm in me!
Swiftly discerning, accomplish what is intensely desired!"

For this, what is the great practice of blessing all the accomplishments of the glorious three samayas of the sublime supreme secret?

In accordance with the yoga of one's own deity,
Blessed by all the mudrās,
Through the supreme great reality itself
Having accomplished oneself, one should recite the heart mantra.

Then, the supremely accomplished worship by the vajra song of the sublime samaya of the great secret of blessing the supreme accomplishment of all the tathāgatas is this:

"oṃ Because you are born from space and bear the marks,
Supreme, without beginning or end,
Of the nature of the great vajra being,
Vajrasattva, grant me accomplishment!
All-Supreme, the great accomplishment,
Maheśvara, the surpassing god,
Vajra Holder, king of all,
Grant me supreme, unchanging accomplishment!
You who are faultless and everlasting,
Passionately attached through passion,
Great passion, great joy,
Blessed One, by that very nature grant me accomplishment!
Utterly pure, supreme of all,
Tathāgata, liberated from the very beginning,"

Samantabhadra, the self of all,
Grant that I be accomplished as a bodhisattva!
Supreme of all, the great accomplishment,
Maheśvara, the surpassing god,
Through great praise of the accomplished vajra,
Vajra Pride, lord, make me the lord!
You, Blessed One of Śrī Paramādya:
Whoever praises you with meaning and with song
Will accomplish the supreme of all accomplishments,
Maheśvara himself."

Thus the Blessed One Vajradhara taught the supremely accomplished great song, supremely secret, of the supreme reality.

Through this rite of accomplishment
One truly realizes Vajrasattva,
And fully accomplishes the very lordship
Of the great accomplishment of the All-Supreme.
Displays of rays of light in every direction and the like,
And the marvels displayed individually,
Through the vajra words, from one's own deity,
Wonders will be seen.
The great variety of all the ritual manuals,
Of the lords of accomplishment and the blissful ones,
Through swiftly blessing oneself,
Is obtained by the vajra master.

Thus spoke the Blessed One Vajrasattva, the Supreme Primordial One.

For this, what is the tantra of the great practice of blessing oneself with all the deities of the glorious three samayas of the supreme secret?

In accordance with the yoga of one's own deity,
Through the bodies that are the self of all the deities,
Through the very self of the yoga of one's own deity,
One should accomplish one's own chosen deity.

Then, here is the elaborate rite, accomplished in accordance, of the sublime accomplishment of the whole host of activities of all the accomplishments, such as the very nature of Maheśvara, the great accomplishment of the All-Supreme, of the glorious three samayas of the sublime supreme secret, and of the sublime accomplishment of all supremely unchanging, sublime, unsurpassed joy, utter joy, bliss and happiness of mind:

In accordance with the yoga of one's own deity,
Through the bodies of the Vajra All-Supreme,
The rite of song and music
Becomes impassioned through the vajra itself.
In accordance with the yoga of one's own deity,
By awakening the root of melody,
The rite of song and music
Is echoed by the bell itself.
In accordance with the yoga of one's own deity,
The supreme lord of the various mudrās:
The rite of song and music
Should be accomplished in accord with the samaya.
Through this, in all the ritual manuals,
Being fully awakened by the descent of the vajra,
The samaya of one's own deity
Will be accomplished fourfold in every case.

Thus the Blessed One Vajrasattva taught the supremely secret, supremely accomplished rite of the great song and of the music of the glorious three samayas of the sublime supreme secret.

Then, following that, according to the place,
With as much as one has and as is comfortable,
Having worshiped with flowers and incense,
In a house, a pleasure grove or the like,
Having fully prepared oneself as one wishes,
Endowed with as many ornaments and garments as one has,
Properly clad in varied clothing,
With a garland of flowers and a fragrant mouth,
Whether cast or hammered,
Or assembled, or drawn as a picture,
Before that primordial circle,
Having invited it to be seated according to the rite,
In accordance with the yoga of one's own deity,
Fully awakening with vajra and bell,
One should accomplish the summoning of all, great worship,
Blessing oneself and the rest.
Having first accomplished this once,
Then, after that, as one wishes,
Whatever one wishes and whatever one says,
One eats everything and does everything.
Whether standing or sitting,
Laughing heartily or speaking,
Just as things are, so, here and there,
One should set them out as one wishes.
The circle of the supreme secret,
Having worshiped it with flowers and the rest,
One should accomplish in accordance, as it is.
Through this one becomes lord of all desires;
One goes and comes, and, if one wishes, flies;
One accomplishes the lordship of all accomplishments;
One becomes a sovereign free of old age and death.
Vajrasattva, the great accomplished one,
Lord, sovereign of all the mudrās,
The king who holds all vajras,
Holds all vajra weapons.
Invincible and unbearable,
Doing all things and the support of all,
The great king of all sentient beings,
He is able to subdue and to take into his care.
The great passion of the All-Supreme,
Fulfillment of desires, supreme bliss:
One becomes accomplished as victorious over all,
The supreme lord of the All-Supreme.
Supreme of all, the great accomplishment,
Abiding in the state of Maheśvara,
The king who holds all vajras,
Will fully accomplish all the buddhas.

Thus, the supremely passionate joy, the great enclosure of lotuses,
Bestows the unsurpassed accomplishment of the supreme secret accomplished.
Whoever relies on it, raising the supreme banner,
Experiences the supreme accomplishment, Maheśvara himself.

Thus spoke the Blessed One Vajrasattva.

From the Śrī Paramādya: the elaborate rite of the glorious three samayas of the sublime great secret, of summoning all accomplishments, of the great worship, of blessing oneself, and the rest.

The supreme secret, delight,
And what all the buddhas have taught:
Vajrasattva, in his own abode,
The self of all, ever abides.
The being without beginning or end
Is Vajrasattva, great joy,
Samantabhadra, the self of all,
In the manner of Vajra Pride, lord of lords.
This is the self-arisen Blessed One,
The surpassing deity, the one alone.

Thus will it be accomplished.

Thus, the supremely passionate joy, the great enclosure of lotuses,
Bestows the unsurpassed accomplishment of the supreme secret.
Whoever relies on it, raising the supreme banner,
Experiences the supreme accomplishment, Great Bliss itself.

Thus spoke the Blessed One Vajrasattva.

From the Śrī Paramādya: the supremely secret maṇḍala of one's own samaya, the glory of all the tathāgatas, the lord of all yogas, the samaya of the joy of the glorious Vajra Amogha of Great Joy, is complete.

"Then I shall explain the elaborate rite for the disciple of the great samaya of all the tathāgatas, and how to take the disciple into one's care." Thus spoke the Blessed One Vajrasattva.

For this, first of all, one should have the disciple generate the mind of awakening by reciting once this sublime, supremely secret mantra of all the tathāgatas: oṃ sarva yoga cittaṃ udpādayāmi. It is also taught that merely by reciting this, one should be understood to be inseparable from all the tathāgatas, irreversible, a bodhisattva great being bearing the seal that overpowers all māras, and indeed a tathāgata.

Having supremely generated
The unsurpassed mind of awakening,
And having set the vajra at the heart,
surata samaya stvaṃ ho
With the heart mantra one should accomplish the vajra as one pleases.
When the vajra is roused with this,
One will accomplish all that one desires.
By rousing it, swiftly
The unsurpassed mind of awakening,
For the sake of the great accomplishment of the All-Supreme,
Becomes Vajrasattva himself.

This supreme teaching was spoken by the Blessed One Vajrasattva.

Having danced, with palms joined at the crown,
Their faces covered with a cloth,
With desire for whatever accomplishment they wish,
All the disciples should be led in.
With the being of whatever accomplishment is wished for,
The disciple should be made to cast a flower.
On whichever mudrā it falls,
That one's goddess should be accomplished.
Then, having removed the face covering,
And having received offerings of every kind,
Through the jewel empowerment of the buddhas
One should clearly bestow the three samayas.

For this, one should empower the disciple with the oath water of the Great Vajra, and should say the following:

"This is your water of hell:
If you transgress the samaya, it burns you.
If you keep the samaya, accomplishment
Is attained through this water of vajra nectar.
The mudrā of vajra and bell:
If, dwelling amid a gathering of people,
You say you have not seen the maṇḍala,
Or, lacking faith, mock and revile it with laughter..."

With these words the oath is proclaimed.

For this, the rite of the jewel empowerment of the buddhas is this. Having empowered the disciple with the vajra water, one sets him in the place of empowerment of the supreme body of all the tathāgatas, and with this mantra, oṃ sarva tathāgata mahāsamaya adhipati stvaṃ abhiṣviñcāmi mahāvajradhara, one should place the vajra in his hand:

"This is all the buddhas themselves,
The vajra of Vajradhara.
The great discipline of Vajradhara
You too must always uphold.
The great and supreme mind of awakening
Of all the tathāgatas
You must never abandon, and must always practice.
This is the utterly secret bliss."

oṃ vajra karmma vādhidhi śrī vajra nāma siddhi samaya stvaṃ bhūr bhuva svaḥ

To the one on whom the vajra name is bestowed, the vocative "O" should be prefixed. That name of his also becomes his name as a tathāgata until he reaches the seat of awakening.

For this, the bestowal of the glorious three samayas of the sublime supreme secret is this:

"The being without beginning or end
Is Vajrasattva, great joy,
Samantabhadra, the self of all,
In the manner of Vajra Pride, lord of lords."

Having thus explained the reality of the five secrets, the reality of the supreme person of the Blessed One Śrī Paramādya, one should bestow the vajra.

This is the supreme command of the Dharma properly bestowed by the Blessed One, the sublime lord of existence, the supreme of all, the sublime among lords, the Tathāgata himself:

"Existence is pure by its very nature;
By nature it is made free of existence.
The supreme being, pure by nature,
Brings about supreme existence."

That which brings down the supremely secret nature of reality is the bell. It should be rung when worshiping all the tathāgatas or when accomplishing the deity. Then, through those strokes that fully awaken the reality of all phenomena, all obscurations are completely purified. It will be practiced as that which brings down all vajras, fully accomplishes, brings under control, and fully gladdens.

Then it is said: "One should always rely on the samaya, the great secret of all the ritual manuals, the supreme means of purifying existence."

For this:

"This is for you, today,
So that the bliss of existence comes most swiftly,
In order to purify great suffering,
The sublime means of utter bliss:
Always generate this samaya.
Relying on all the enjoyments of desire
Just as you wish,
Through the yoga of your own deity,
Make offering to yourself and to others."

The yoga of the samaya of the unsurpassed, supremely secret Great Vehicle, greater even than the great, of all the tathāgatas, is the samaya of the Blessed One.

"Since even all the tathāgatas
Never transgress it,
It is proclaimed the samaya of the mudrā,
Because it makes firm the body of the mind."

Thus spoke the Blessed One Vajrasattva, the Supremely Secret.

For this, one should say the following:

"As long as the supreme sage
Remains in the abode of saṃsāra,
For so long he is able to accomplish the aims of beings without equal,
Not passing into nirvāṇa.
The sublime secret, the means of the samaya,
Accomplished with the blessing of merit:
Always the lord of all accomplishments,
He accomplishes the aims of beings.
One who accomplishes the supreme through this samaya,
Accomplishing it as one pleases,
Even if one commits misdeeds, will not be harmed
In any way by any evil.
Great desire will be accomplished.
The lord of the samaya, the lord of all,
The king who holds all vajras,
Is the supreme lord of the All-Supreme.
Through the accomplishment of the host of activities,
By accomplishing them entirely, one succeeds.
By sealing oneself and all others,
Or else for all that is to be accomplished,
There is supreme accomplishment that is never in vain,
And one accomplishes all yogas in accord.
The glorious one, who steals away all suffering:
The samaya is hard to transgress."

Thus spoke the Blessed One Vajrasattva, the Supremely Secret.

By obtaining and holding the vajra and the rest,
All the bliss of every accomplishment
Comes down, and the vajra disciple
Experiences it within the maṇḍala.
Then, through the praise of all exalting him,
Being in truth the very self of all the buddhas,
Through the empowerment with a vajra name,
He should be prophesied as a tathāgata.

For this, the rite of Vajrasattva for the prophecy by all the tathāgatas is this:

"The great mudrā of all the buddhas:
Whoever is prophesied with exalting praise,
That one is all the buddhas themselves,
Empowered with a vajra name."

"oṃ! Now I prophesy you:
Vajrasattva, Tathāgata!
Because existence is perfectly accomplished,
I show you the way out of the evil destinies of existence.
he vajra namaḥ tathāgata siddhi samaya stvaṃ bhūr bhuva svaḥ."

Whoever receives this exalting praise and this prophecy as the great mudrā, performed by the supremely secret rite, is prophesied to unsurpassed, perfect and complete awakening by all the tathāgatas beginning with Vajrasattva, together with the maṇḍala of bodhisattvas including Vajradhara, speaking with one voice with the masters of the personal pledge (samaya) of all the tathāgatas. That is to say, one should have faith in the great mudrā of this very exalting praise, in this blessing through the accomplishment of the supremely secret and sublime, and in the power of mantra.

Then, to those who have entered in the proper way, one should give the encouragement of the assembly of all the tathāgatas and so forth:

"All the tathāgatas, however many there are, together with Vajradhara and with the maṇḍala of bodhisattvas, all of them, out of attachment to their own pledge, have entered here completely: through the very stability of the entire vajra realm of summoning, causing to enter, bringing under control, accomplishing and further accomplishing, and through the all-pervasiveness of the entire realm of phenomena by which this becomes stable. Through the vastness of the entire element of space they are seated here, and well seated. Each and every one of them, focusing especially on the very state of a tathāgata that depends on the personal pledge of all the tathāgatas, has blessed you with the words, 'This is all the tathāgatas, the master of the personal pledge of all the tathāgatas.' All the tathāgatas, the masters of their own pledge, with the one voice of the pledge, are encouraging you. Have faith!"

This is the supremely secret encouragement of all the tathāgatas:

"By seeing and by entering
The sublime secret maṇḍala,
You are definitively freed from all wrongdoing,
And you abide well in that very place.
From this vehicle of Great Bliss
There is no dying again;
Unassailable and unslayable,
Fearing nothing at all, take your delight!
The reversal of the suffering of existence
Comes because existence is perfectly accomplished;
You, who have become the supreme Teacher,
Are existence perfectly accomplished.
This accomplished pledge and vow
You must always guard.
All the buddhas proclaimed it in harmony;
It is the sublime and eternal command.
The awakening mind, by whose mere arising
Buddhahood is beyond all doubt,
Should never be given up.
Whatever is the mudrā and the vajra,
And the sublime Dharma, do not revile,
And never cast them aside.
Out of ignorance or delusion
You should never revile them.
Do not forsake your own self
And torment it with austerities;
Take bliss, as blissfully as you please:
This one is a perfect buddha yet to come.
The vajra, the bell and the mudrās
You should never abandon.
Do not revile the master,
For he is like all the buddhas.
Whoever reviles the master,
The guru equal to all the buddhas,
Reviles all the buddhas thereby,
And will always meet with suffering:
Tormented by fevers, by poison and compounded poisons,
And by the ḍākinīs,
Slain by fierce grahas and vināyakas,
That being falls into hell.
Therefore, with every effort,
The vajra master of great intelligence,
Who makes no boast of his virtue,
Should never be reviled.
To the guru, with respect,
You should give a fitting offering;
By that, fevers and other harms
Will never arise again.
The master of one's own pledge
Should always be served even with what one would not give,
With sons and wife and one's own life;
What need to mention fleeting wealth?
For he it is who grants to the diligent,
In this very life,
Buddhahood itself, so hard to find
Even in countless koṭis of eons.
Always guard your own pledge;
Always worship the tathāgatas;
Always make offerings to the guru as well,
For he is like all the buddhas.
To give to him
Is always to give to all the buddhas.
To give to him is the accumulation of merit,
And from the accumulation comes the supreme accomplishment.
Therefore, by abiding well in this,
Today your birth has borne its fruit.
Equal to the self-arisen deities of the pledge
Today you will become, beyond doubt.
Since all the buddhas, together with the Vajra Holder,
Have all empowered you today,
You are revealed as the great king of the three realms,
The lord of kings.
Such a one is victorious over Māra
And has entered the supreme city.
That you will attain buddhahood itself
Here today, there is no doubt."

Thus spoke, in supreme truth, the Blessed One Vajrasattva, together with all the tathāgatas, Vajradhara, and the maṇḍala of the retinue of great bodhisattvas.

Then this is the supremely secret encouragement of all the tathāgatas, given by the glorious master of the personal pledge of all the tathāgatas and by the assemblies of disciples:

"Through the mudrā of irreversibility,
Arisen together with the awakening mind,
Buddhahood itself is to be realized."

So it is said. What is the reason for this? It is because the whole being of each of you becomes irreversible merely by the power of having generated the great awakening mind of all the tathāgatas. Because you are irreversible, you abide in the group of those who are certain (niyata), and so you are certain of unsurpassed, perfect and complete awakening. It should be understood that you will become fully and perfectly awakened into unsurpassed, perfect and complete awakening. So it is said.

Then this is the supremely secret encouragement of the master of the pledge, the glory of all:

"Uniting oneself as Vajrasattva,
Endowed with all the yogas and their subsequent practice,
One is to be realized as buddhahood itself;
This the wise should examine supremely."

So it is said.

What is the reason for this? It is because the whole being of each of you has, beyond any doubt, taken up the personal pledge of all the tathāgatas. You should be understood as tathāgatas, fully awakened into unsurpassed, perfect and complete awakening. Through following in practice the glorious personal pledge of all the tathāgatas, those supreme accomplishments should be understood as being all the tathāgatas themselves.

Therefore, in every circumstance, as far as you are able, giving no opening to any suffering or unhappiness, you should guard your own whole being, and you should not destroy it. With all enjoyments and requisites whatsoever, you should make perfect offering through the practice of the pledge of the supremely secret, unsurpassed Great Vehicle of all the tathāgatas, take hold of bliss, and make it increase fully. Just as one strives earnestly for oneself, so one generates the same in return for others.

Because there is the danger of falling into wrong views, into the outer tīrthikas, and into the level of all the śrāvakas and pratyekabuddhas, this body is kept utterly secret; speech, by not speaking at all; and mind, by complete cessation. It was also taught that through this your vajras of body, speech and mind will always come forth as the vajra of body, speech and mind of all the tathāgatas, the supreme and unsurpassed accomplishment of their glorious personal pledge.

"With all the enjoyments of desire,
Fearing nothing at all, take your delight!
This pledge is hard to transgress;
Being without wrongdoing, do not fear!"

Thus the Blessed One Vajrasattva spoke in supreme truth, and it is the intent of all the tathāgatas together with the maṇḍala of the retinue of bodhisattvas.

"Thus make your mind serene and adamantine;
Show forth your own pledge, inexhaustible, the giver of bliss.
Realize that it brings swift bliss to beings,
Is perfectly equal to Vajrasattva, and is eternal.
Then with flavors, food, conduct, dance and song,
With offerings of music and the like, worship the Protector.
When the great vajra guru has offered these,
Make offerings to yourself and to others as you wish.
Partaking as you wish
Of drinks that intoxicate and that do not,
With the rites of song and music and the rest,
Perform the recitation that follows along accordingly.
With songs, music and dances
Taken up through the rite of one's own secret,
With the offering of the various mudrās,
And with the music of Vajrasattva and the like,
Accompanied by women and others,
Or together with one's own disciples,
By making offerings with enjoyments as one wishes,
One swiftly attains accomplishment."

Thus spoke the supremely secret Blessed One Vajrasattva.

Here the distinctive songs of the secret rite are these:
hūṃ is of the Blessed One, the supreme first Vajrasattva.
a is of the tathāgatas.
hi is of the glorious Victory over the Three Worlds.
oṃ is of the Lotus Essence.
ha is Ākāśagarbha, that is, Ākāśagarbha, king of the great bodhisattvas.
So it was said.

"The songs of one's own secret essence
Are to be performed with the five syllables.
This, taken up through the rite of one's own secret,
Belongs to all the supremely gone ones."

Then the supremely secret offering of vajra song:

"With vajra and bell, cymbals and the like,
Play music in solitude.
Songs sung through the rite of one's own secret
Are fully accomplished with secret music."

Then the supremely and utterly secret offering of vajra dance:

"Having performed the dance
Of the great grasped object together with the grasping subject,
And having performed a dance in the form of the maṇḍala,
Make offering to yourself by circling all around."

Then the supremely secret offering of the various mudrās:

"The rites and magical displays, all of them,
Of the deities, Vajrasattva and the rest:
Whatever dance there is with tales, emblems and mudrās,
That is the music.
With faces of one's own deities
Made of gold, or else of silver,
Or likewise made of wood,
Perform the dances with accomplished mudrās.
Whatever maṇḍala one practices,
Through the offering of the various mudrās,
Have each and every assembly of disciples
Recite, and admit them all in the supreme way.
Through this rite of variety
One is fully freed from all wrongdoing;
All merit too increases,
And one is always protected.
Fevers, grahas, poisons and illness,
Compounded poisons, the harm of ḍākinīs,
Māras and those who kill:
By this they are completely pacified.
Accomplishments become abundant,
And likewise joy and lordship;
And the sublime yoga
That is the nature of all the buddhas is attained."

Thus spoke the utterly secret Blessed One Vajrasattva.

Then, having proclaimed the extensive rite of the offering of the various mudrās and the extensive rite of the vajra-song offering for entering the supreme secret of each and every one of the special deities, there follows what is called the supreme accomplishment of the great pledge of the variety of all the rites, performed with songs and music composed by the vajra master, or with any others one likes.

First of all, the song offering for entering the supremely secret pledge of the Blessed One Śrī Paramādya Vajrasattva is this:

"Mind of sublime bliss, passionately devoted to all,
Vajrasattva, you are the pledge of supreme joy:
May Great Bliss become firm, exalted and clear!
Discerning each one, turn toward me and swiftly accomplish!"

Then the vajra-song offering for entering the supremely secret pledge of the family of all the tathāgatas:

"Through gathering in Vajrasattva,
Unsurpassed Vajraratna;
Through singing of Vajradharma,
Vajrakarma comes to be performed."

Then the vajra-song offering for entering the supremely secret pledge of the entire vajra family:

"hūṃ Vajra with bow and arrow, I bow to you!
Glorious pledge of vajra passion, I bow to you!
khaṭvāṃ, vajra of the time of destruction, I bow to you!
Glory who rings the vajra bell, I bow to you!"

Then the vajra-song offering for entering the supremely secret pledge of the entire lotus family:

"At times a body utterly fierce, robbing the life of savage people;
At times, to tame infinite beings, a body holding a razor-sharp wheel;
At times a body utterly peaceful on a lotus seat, unchanging;
At times, utterly blazing pledge of desire, Lotus King, accomplish me!"

Then the vajra-song offering for entering the supremely secret pledge of the entire jewel family:

"From the sublime Īśvara of all empowerments,
Perfectly accomplished, supreme among inexhaustible treasures;
Through raising the victorious banner of the All-Supreme,
Glorious Vajraratna, you have been well worshipped by me."

Then, of the three brothers: bhūr bhuva svaḥ. Then, of the four sisters: hrī hūṃ śrī bhyo. Then, of the maṇḍala of all the nāga families: phu phu.

Then the vajra-song offering of the maṇḍala of all the wicked ones of Vajrapāṇi, the great general of the yakṣas: sarba maṃga maṇḍal midaṃ vajra ṅharā krama bimarda bikir bhanja udsādaya nāśaya hana daha paca bhasmi kuṃru kroda hūṃkāra mantriṇa.

Then the supremely secret vajra-song offering of the pledge of the great maṇḍala of the sublime Īśvara, supreme among all:

"At times your body is beautiful with the light of a thousand stainless moon disks;
At times your mass of rays, like ten million suns rising at the time of destruction, blazes in great terror;
At times you hold the body of a Victor, radiant and shining, blissful and peaceful;
At times, bearing a supremely beautiful form, you hold a bow together with the supreme arrow;
At times, Vajrasattva, striving to purify those to be tamed, you abide like space.
Since you are the sublime bliss skilled in taming all without exception, may it be accomplished for me!"

Then this is the supreme accomplishment of the vajra-song offering for entering the glorious triple pledge, the supreme secret of the supreme sublime of the supremely secret:

"Into the city of liberation, delightful with the bliss of all accomplishments,
Enters the Blessed One, Great Bliss.
Through the accomplishment of supreme and sublime bliss,
jaḥ hūṃ baṃ hoḥrab: grant perfect accomplishment!"

Then, having proclaimed what is called the utterly supreme accomplishment of the utterly and supremely secret vajra-song offering of the glorious unsurpassed pledge, the unsurpassed, sublime and supremely secret rite of the utterly and supremely secret rite, and having followed it in practice exactly as it is, through the rite of songs and dances of the vajra-song offering of utterly supreme reality, one should practice each in turn. And it was also said, "This is the utterly supreme accomplishment of the offering of the great pledge of the variety of all the unsurpassed and utterly, supremely secret rites of all the tathāgatas."

"Through this rite of variety
Vajrasattva is gathered in,
And the lord of the yoga
